# A critical comparison of the Xylander and Vinet translations

Russian and English versions A–D · OpenAI GPT-6 (Codex) · 2026-11-20

Three subagents drafted groups 1–3. The lead reviewer drafted group 4. Each group then received a second reading by a different reviewer. The lead editor assembled and validated both formats. Reference labels distinguish direct checks from recollection. Internet access was available.

[Xylander](#xylander) · [Vinet](#vinet) · [Term concordance](#term-concordance) · [Overall assessment](#overall-assessment)

## Xylander

### X01 · Xyl. 40.1–2

*Exacta summa hic, evidensque Musicae.*

**Sense.** A compact title announcing a precise, clear summary of music, here.

**Russian base · D.** The verbal title conveys the compendious character of summa and marks the supplied noun.

**English base · D.** “Precise and lucid summary” gives both adjectives their natural force in a title.

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · The heading supplies no author's name. The running title COMPENDIVM on the facing page helps explain summa.

### X02 · Xyl. 40.3–7

*Universum hoc, antiqui Musica contineri perhibuerunt. Nihil enim in rerum natura absque commensuratione et proportione consistit:*

**Sense.** The ancients said that music holds this universe together: nothing in nature subsists without commensuration and proportion.

**Russian base · D.** The active construction makes the cosmic function of music immediately intelligible.

**English base · C.** Preserves both members of the recurring pair and the opening emphasis on the universe.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**C · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** VNiuersum hoc, antiqui Musica / contineri perhibuerunt. Nihil e- / nim in rerum natura absque com- / mensuratione et proportione con- / sistit: neque quicquam eorum quæ  
**Proposal.** Эта вселенная, как утверждали древние, заключена в музыке. / This universe, the ancients declared, is contained in music.  
The bare ablative gives the means by which things hold together. X04 calls music the universe’s harmony and X07 describes it ordering all things. Spatial enclosure obscures that argument.  
Basel 1556, 40.3–7, 13–15 [checked] · Allen and Greenough §409 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Keep the lexical distinction between commensuratio, proportio and the proportionalitas of X04, especially ahead of the numerical use of proportio.

### X03 · Xyl. 40.7–12

*neque quicquam eorum quae fiunt, sine debita commensuratione et proportione si fiat, recte productum natumve esse dicitur: sive id ab arte efficiatur, sive a natura: et sive sub sensum illud cadat, sive solo intellectu perspiciatur.*

**Sense.** Nothing that comes about without due commensuration and proportion is said to have been rightly produced or born, whether by art or nature, whether perceived by sense or by intellect alone.

**Russian base · D.** The restrictions of the conditional and both pairs of alternatives are clear in idiomatic Russian.

**English base · D.** The relative clause preserves the condition without suspending the governing verb across a long parenthesis.

#### Corrections

**B · ru · minor language** · confirmed  
“ничто из того, что возникает, нельзя назвать”  
The initial nominative-sounding ничто jars with the impersonal нельзя назвать.  
**Correction.** Use «ни о чём из возникающего нельзя сказать» or «ничего из того, что возникает, нельзя назвать».

### X04 · Xyl. 40.13–15

*Caeterum Musica ipsa per se commensuratio quaedam omnino et proportionalitas est, nimirum harmonia universi existens.*

**Sense.** Music itself, taken in its own right, is indeed a kind of commensuration and proportionality, since it is the harmony of the universe.

**Russian base · B.** Keeps ipsa per se and separates the assertion from the explanatory clause.

**English base · D.** Distinguishes proportionality from proportion and reads omnino naturally with the assertion.

#### 1. Does omnino mark extent or assent?

*quaedam omnino*

**A, C** · Entirely, altogether.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | всецело некая соразмерность | altogether a certain commensuration |
| C | всецело некая соразмерность | altogether a kind of commensuration |

**B, D** · Certainly, assuredly.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | несомненно, есть своего рода соразмерность | assuredly a kind of commensurateness |
| D | без сомнения, некая соразмерность | certainly a kind of commensuration |

**Verdict.** The extensive sense of omnino is well attested. Lewis and Short also gives an assertive force, especially in concessions. In this positive assertion quaedam qualifies the kind of commensuration and nimirum introduces a further affirmation. ‘Certainly a kind’ reads naturally; ‘altogether’ is defensible if it qualifies the identification as a whole.

**Russian.** несомненно, есть своего рода соразмерность  
**English.** is assuredly a kind of commensuration

Lewis & Short, s.v. omnino [checked] · Basel 1556, 40.13–15 [checked] · medium confidence · modified · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**D · ru · minor omission** · confirmed  
“Сама же музыка есть”  
Сама conveys ipsa, but the further emphasis per se disappears.  
**Correction.** Use «Сама же музыка по себе» or «Музыка же сама по себе».

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**B · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** Cæterùm Musica ipsa per se commensura- / tio quædam omnino et proportionalitas est, ni- / mirum harmonia uniuersi existens. Fortassis et  
**Proposal.** Сама музыка по себе во всех отношениях есть своего рода соразмерность и пропорциональность. / Music itself is in every respect a kind of commensurateness and proportionality.  
The extensive force is lexical, although the assertive force sits more naturally with quaedam.  
Lewis & Short, s.v. omnino [checked] · Basel 1556, 40.13–15 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **A** · Proportionalitas replaces proportio in X04. The terminological shift can be retained without insisting that the author here specifies a relation between two ratios.

#### For the editor

- Should omnino mark assent or extent in the definition?

### X05 · Xyl. 40.15–20

*Fortassis et ipsam divinam naturam haud inconvenienter sui ipsius et universi harmoniam quispiam dixerit: siquidem in Deo concinnatus, et ad consonantiam coaptatus mundus, bene, atque adeo quam optime se habere videtur.*

**Sense.** One might fittingly call even the divine nature the harmony of itself and the universe. The world, ordered in God and fitted to consonance, appears to be in the best possible condition.

**Russian base · A.** The escalating bene … quam optime and the potential of dixerit remain audible.

**English base · C.** Attuned and fitted differentiate the two verbs, with videtur and the superlative intact.

#### Corrections

**B · en · minor omission** · added  
“Perhaps one might without impropriety call the divine nature itself”  
In the English, ‘itself’ renders ipsam, but the additive et (‘even/also’) has no counterpart.  
**Correction.** even the divine nature itself

**D · en · minor omission** · added  
“One might perhaps aptly call the divine nature itself”  
In the English, ‘itself’ renders ipsam, but the additive et (‘even/also’) has no counterpart.  
**Correction.** even the divine nature itself

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · The pair concinnare/coaptare prepares the account of musical fitting in X12. Keep the difference visible alongside the technical consonantia.

### X06 · Xyl. 40.20–21

*Tanto nimirum Musicam illi dignati sunt miraculo.*

**Sense.** The ancients evidently held music worthy of such wonder.

**Russian base · C.** Miraculo is the admiration accorded, and разумеется reflects nimirum.

**English base · B = D.** Both give the same idiomatic expression for the ancients’ wonder.

#### Corrections

**A · en · minor language** · confirmed  
“Of so great a wonder, evidently, did they deem Music worthy.”  
The inversion and indefinite article make the observer’s wonder sound like an object or miraculous event.  
**Correction.** “Such, evidently, was the wonder they thought music deserved.”

**C · en · minor language** · confirmed  
“Of so great a wonder, indeed, did they deem music worthy.”  
An indefinite wonder suggests a remarkable object and obscures the attitude expressed by dignari.  
**Correction.** “Such, indeed, was the wonder they thought music deserved.”

**B · en · minor omission** · added  
“Such was the wonder they thought music deserved.”  
The English omits nimirum, which emphasizes the inference drawn from the preceding description.  
**Correction.** Such, evidently, was the wonder they thought music deserved.

**D · en · minor omission** · added  
“Such was the wonder they thought music deserved.”  
The English omits nimirum, which emphasizes the inference drawn from the preceding description.  
**Correction.** Such, evidently, was the wonder they thought music deserved.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · sound** · modified  
**Printed.** optimè se habere uidetur. Tanto nimirum Mu- / sicam illi dignati sunt miraculo. Ea quando in  
**Proposal.** ‘honoured music with so great admiration’  
Dignari takes what someone is judged worthy of in the ablative. Wonder as the response of the ancients gives a coherent compliment to music. An object of marvel does not fit as well after ‘worthy of’.  
Allen and Greenough §418b note 1 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. miraculum [checked] · Basel 1556, 40.20–21 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The construction with miraculum can express an observer’s wonder. Pliny’s tree worthy of marvel supplies a useful comparison.

### X07 · Xyl. 40.21–41.3

*Ea quando in omnibus conspicitur, atque universa temperat, exornatque, tum sensui voluptatem, congruentiam autem (harmoniam dico) Naturae, beatitatem denique intellectui largitur: nos de ea Musica quae in sensibus, aut potius uno sensuum auditu consideratur, paucis disseremus.*

**Sense.** Music is discernible in everything, regulates and adorns all, and gives pleasure to sense, congruence (harmony) to nature, and blessedness to intellect. We shall speak briefly of music as considered by the senses, more precisely by hearing.

**Russian base · D.** Communicates the three recipients and their gifts clearly and translates temperat in context.

**English base · B.** Connects all three gifts with the causal introduction and preserves music as an object of consideration.

#### 1. Is music perceived or considered under the aspect of sense?

*in sensibus … consideratur*

**A, B, C** · The subject is considered or examined in, or through, the senses.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | которая рассматривается в чувствах | which is considered in the senses |
| B | которую рассматривают посредством чувств | as considered by the senses |
| C | которая рассматривается в чувствах | which is considered in the senses |

**D** · The subject is perceived by the senses.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | которую воспринимают чувствами | that is perceived by the senses |

**Verdict.** Consideratur can mean observed or perceived, as Lewis and Short records, as well as considered intellectually. The coming exposition and the limitation to one sense favour ‘examined through hearing’. D emphasizes the sensory object and is defensible in this setting.

**Russian.** о той музыке, которую рассматривают с точки зрения чувственного восприятия, вернее, слуха  
**English.** the music studied through the senses, or rather through one of them, hearing

Lewis & Short, s.v. considero [checked] · Basel 1556, 40.21–41.3 [checked] · medium confidence · modified · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**C · en · minor omission** · added  
“the one sense of hearing”  
The genitive plural sensuum marks hearing as one of the senses. ‘The one sense of hearing’ blurs the selection from that plurality.  
**Correction.** one of the senses, hearing

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** sicam illi dignati sunt miraculo. Ea quando in / omnibus conspicitur, atque uniuersa temperat, e- / xornatqúe, tum sensui uoluptatem, congruentiam / autem (harmoniam dico) Naturæ, beatitatem / denique intellectui largitur: nos de ea Musica quæ / in sensibus, aut potius uno sensuum auditu con- / sideratur, paucis disseremus.  
**Proposal.** ‘since it... and bestows’ / ‘when it..., then it bestows’  
Quando and tum can form a temporal correlation, but the universal claims describe a standing relation. Causal quando better introduces the decision to discuss audible music.  
Lewis & Short, s.v. quando, temporal and causal conjunction [checked] · Basel 1556, 40.21–41.3 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Congruentia is glossed as harmonia here. Vinet uses convenientia at the corresponding point.

### X08 · Xyl. 41.4–5

*Sonus est, vocis nullo intervallo divisae harmoniae particeps intensio, seu tenor.*

**Sense.** A note is the pitch or tenor of a voice undivided by any interval, capable of participating in harmony. The placement of the genitive and of particeps allows more than one attachment.

**Russian base · B.** Names the musical quantity as pitch and keeps the qualifier on that pitch.

**English base · D.** “Pitch, or steady tone” makes the paired definition intelligible and resolves the attachment.

#### 1. What do the two near-equivalents designate?

*intensio, seu tenor*

**A** · Tension or an act of holding.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | напряжение, или удержание | a tension, or sustained holding |

**C** · Tension or pitch.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | напряжение, или высота | the tension, or pitch |

**B, D** · The pitch or sustained tone of a voice.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | высота, или выдерживаемый тон | a pitch, or sustained tone |
| D | высота голоса | the pitch, or steady tone |

**Verdict.** A note here has a determinate pitch. The etymological idea of tension belongs in an explanatory note. Tenor can describe a sustained course and a vocal tone. No separate pitch interval arises while it is held.

**Russian.** высота, или выдерживаемый тон, голоса  
**English.** the pitch, or sustained tone, of a voice

Boethius, De institutione musica I.8, Friedlein p. 195 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. tenor, Quintilian 1.5.22, 26 [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**A · both · minor mistranslation** · modified  
“напряжение, или удержание, голоса / a tension, or sustained holding, of a voice”  
‘Tension’ is historically intelligible, and ‘undivided by any interval’ limits the voice to a single level. The combination with ‘holding’ leaves the primary musical quantity, pitch, hard to recognize in both languages.  
**Correction.** Use «высота, или выдерживаемый тон» / “pitch, or sustained tone”.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · modified  
**Printed.** Sonus est, uocis nullo interuallo diuisæ har- / moniæ particeps intensio, seu tenor. Interuallum  
**Proposal.** Звук есть напряжение, или удержание, голоса, причастное гармонии, не разделённой никаким интервалом. / A note is a tension, or sustained holding, of the voice that partakes of a harmony divided by no interval.  
Divisae could agree in form with either genitive, but X12 makes the individual note indivisible. Moreover the English alternative makes the voice participate, although particeps is nominative, not genitive participis. X11 defines harmony as a construction of systems.  
Basel 1556, 41.4–13 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.8, Friedlein p. 195 [checked]

**B · doubt · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** Sonus est, uocis nullo interuallo diuisæ har- / moniæ particeps intensio, seu tenor. Interuallum  
**Proposal.** Звук — причастная гармонии высота, или устойчивый уровень высоты, голосового звучания, не разделённого никаким интервалом. / A note is a pitch, or steady level of pitch, of vocal sound undivided by any interval, which participates in harmony.  
A steady pitch level is an intelligible result of holding the voice. Preserve participation in harmony as a property of the pitch.  
Lewis & Short, s.v. tenor, Quintilian 1.5.22, 26 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.8, Friedlein p. 195 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **A** · Use the three distinctions in the note: a single pitch, absence of internal interval, and capacity to be fitted to another note. Boethius I.8 supplies a close Latin parallel.

### X09 · Xyl. 41.5–7

*Intervallum autem, sonorum mutua unius ad alterum ratio, sive respectus.*

**Sense.** An interval is the mutual relation or reference of notes, one to another.

**Russian base · D.** Makes the relation between two notes explicit without tortuous word order.

**English base · D.** Places the two notes on either side of the ratio and retains the broader gloss ‘relation’.

#### Corrections

**A · notes · minor note-claim** · added  
“a double rendering of one term”  
Sive presents respectus as a gloss of ratio in this Latin. The claim that a single word underlies them cannot be verified from this version.  
**Correction.** Describe Xylander's alternative expressions. The conjecture about the underlying wording is not verified.

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Here respectus glosses ratio. Vinet’s word in the corresponding definition is habitudo.

### X10 · Xyl. 41.7–8

*Systema, complexus est certus intervallorum.*

**Sense.** A system is a definite combination of intervals.

**Russian base · B = C = D.** The three texts coincide and render the definite combination succinctly.

**English base · B = C = D.** The three texts give the same precise definition.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**C · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** siue respectus. Systema, complexus est certus / interuallorum. Harmonia, ipsa constructio sy-  
**Proposal.** Система — некое сочетание интервалов. / A system is a certain combination of intervals.  
An unspecified but particular combination is possible. Certus more naturally indicates that the combination has a determinate constitution.  
Lewis & Short, s.v. certus [checked] · Basel 1556, 41.7–8 [checked]

### X11 · Xyl. 41.8–9

*Harmonia, ipsa constructio systematum.*

**Sense.** Harmony is the very construction of systems.

**Russian base · A = B = C = D.** All four give the same Russian definition and retain ipsa.

**English base · A = C = D.** The identical phrase ‘the very construction’ reproduces the emphasis of ipsa.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The construction of systems anticipates X45–47, where their combination is described as the work of harmony.

### X12 · Xyl. 41.9–13

*Enimvero sonus divisionis capax non est, ut neque punctum in Geometria. Harmoniae participem diximus, quia ita effertur, ut possit etiam cum alio sono ad harmoniam concinnari.*

**Sense.** A note admits no division, any more than a geometrical point. Its utterance can be fitted harmoniously to another note, hence the qualification about participation in harmony.

**Russian base · D.** Connects the note’s indivisibility with the geometrical point, then explains its relation to a second note.

**English base · D.** “Sounded” and “fitted to another note” give precise, natural musical English.

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Euclid’s definition of the point explains the comparison: the object is defined as having no part.

### X13 · Xyl. 41.13–16

*Quod nisi fiat, iam is dissonus sit sonus, non consonus: imo neque sonus dicetur esse, sed strepitus quispiam temere editus, et ad consonantiam nulli existens usui.*

**Sense.** Otherwise it would be a dissonant, unconsonant note, indeed mere noise emitted at random and of no use for consonance.

**Russian base · D.** Clearly separates the two adjectives and the stronger exclusion of mere noise.

**English base · A.** Preserves the conditional force of sit and the future dicetur and distinguishes sonus from strepitus.

#### Corrections

**B · en · minor mistranslation** · added  
“dissonant, with no consonance”  
The paired adjectives dissonus and non consonus both predicate a quality of the note. The second adjective has become a free-standing abstract noun.  
**Correction.** dissonant, not consonant

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The contrast limits the technical sonus to something that can participate in harmony. Strepitus describes the excluded noise.

### X14 · Xyl. 41.16–25

*Proinde diversae sunt sonorum, quas illi progressu et ordine, quem a gravi ad acutum observant, nanciscuntur nomenclationes: Proslambanomenos, Hypate hypaton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hypate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, diatonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite hyperbolaeon, diatonos hyperbolaeon, nete hyperbolaeon.*

**Sense.** The notes acquire different names from their progression and order from low to high. The fifteen names include the distinctive hypate diatonos, mese monos and paramesos.

**Russian base · C.** Keeps the fifteen positions, the unusual fourth name and the paramesos distinction. The qualifier of mese needs an editorial choice.

**English base · C.** Retains the printed mese monos in a complete list.

#### 1. A double name, an adjective meaning ‘single’, or a comment on the name?

*mese monos*

**A** · Transliterates both words.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | меса монос | mese monos |

**C** · Translates the qualifier as ‘sole’ in Russian, retains both words in English.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | меса единственная | mese monos |

**B, D** · Reads the second word as a comment: simply mese.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | просто меса | simply mese |
| D | просто меса | simply mese |

**Verdict.** Print gives monos without a break before it. The Greek masculine form does not normally qualify feminine mese as an agreeing adjective. Reproducing the two words best preserves what Xylander wrote. Vinet’s own solum makes the gloss plausible but cannot settle Xylander’s intention.

**Russian.** меса монос  
**English.** mese monos

Basel 1556, 41.19–25 [checked] · LSJ, s.v. μόνος [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein pp. 206–208 [checked] · Project conventions §4.4 [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**A · notes · major note-claim** · confirmed  
“mese alone bears no tetrachord name”  
Proslambanomenos and paramesos in this very list also lack a tetrachord name.  
**Correction.** Present ‘simply mese’ as a possible gloss and remove the exclusivity claim.

**C · notes · major note-claim** · confirmed  
“the mese is the one note without a tetrachord qualifier”  
Proslambanomenos and paramesos in this very list also lack a tetrachord name.  
**Correction.** Present ‘simply mese’ as a possible gloss and remove the exclusivity claim.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** tiam nulli existens usui. Proinde diuersæ sunt / sonorum, quas illi progressu et ordine, quem à / graui ad acutum obseruant, nanciscuntur no- / menclationes: Proslambanomenos, Hypate hy- / paton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hy- / pate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, / mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, dia- / tonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite / hyperbolæon, diatonos hyperbolæon, nete hy- / perbolæon. Per has sonorum species heccæde  
**Proposal.** меса одиночная / the single mese  
The sense ‘single/alone’ is lexical, but the masculine monos and the absence of punctuation leave its status uncertain.  
Basel 1556, 41.19–25 [checked] · LSJ, s.v. μόνος [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein pp. 206–208 [checked] · Project conventions §4.4 [checked]

**B · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** tiam nulli existens usui. Proinde diuersæ sunt / sonorum, quas illi progressu et ordine, quem à / graui ad acutum obseruant, nanciscuntur no- / menclationes: Proslambanomenos, Hypate hy- / paton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hy- / pate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, / mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, dia- / tonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite / hyperbolæon, diatonos hyperbolæon, nete hy- / perbolæon. Per has sonorum species heccæde  
**Proposal.** одна лишь меса / mese alone  
The sense ‘single/alone’ is lexical, but the masculine monos and the absence of punctuation leave its status uncertain.

**Printed.** tiam nulli existens usui. Proinde diuersæ sunt / sonorum, quas illi progressu et ordine, quem à / graui ad acutum obseruant, nanciscuntur no- / menclationes: Proslambanomenos, Hypate hy- / paton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hy- / pate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, / mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, dia- / tonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite / hyperbolæon, diatonos hyperbolæon, nete hy- / perbolæon. Per has sonorum species heccæde  
**Proposal.** …диатоническая средних, меса — одна, парамес… / …diatonos meson, the mese alone, paramesos…  
The sense ‘single/alone’ is lexical, but the masculine monos and the absence of punctuation leave its status uncertain.  
Basel 1556, 41.19–25 [checked] · LSJ, s.v. μόνος [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein pp. 206–208 [checked] · Project conventions §4.4 [checked]

**D · doubt · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** tiam nulli existens usui. Proinde diuersæ sunt / sonorum, quas illi progressu et ordine, quem à / graui ad acutum obseruant, nanciscuntur no- / menclationes: Proslambanomenos, Hypate hy- / paton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hy- / pate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, / mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, dia- / tonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite / hyperbolæon, diatonos hyperbolæon, nete hy- / perbolæon. Per has sonorum species heccæde  
**Proposal.** меса монос / mese monos  
This reproduces the actual two-word sequence in the list and postpones interpretation.  
Basel 1556, 41.19–25 [checked] · LSJ, s.v. μόνος [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein pp. 206–208 [checked] · Project conventions §4.4 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Identify the fourth position and the upper diatonic notes by their standard counterparts. Preserve hypate diatonos, singular diezeugmene and the distinction between Xylander’s paramesos and Vinet’s paramese.

#### For the editor

- Should monos stand transliterated in both languages, or should the commentary's tentative interpretation enter the Russian text?

### X15 · Xyl. 41.25–42.5

*Per has sonorum species heccaedecachordum, hoc est, sedecim chordarum instrumentum in quindecim fidibus instructo organo (Pentecaedecachordum vocant Graeci) absolvitur, fine octachordi ad principium sequentium continuo assumpto.*

**Sense.** These note species complete a heccaedecachord, glossed as a sixteen-string instrument, in an instrument fitted with fifteen strings (Greek pentecaedecachord). The octave’s end is taken straight on as the beginning of what follows.

**Russian base · C.** Keeps both instrument names, the count of strings and the bracketed head supplied to sequentium.

**English base · C.** Retains and glosses the two instrument names while identifying the shared octachord boundary.

#### 1. Does the unusual first name designate an instrument or a separate theoretical system?

*heccaedecachordum, hoc est, sedecim chordarum instrumentum*

**A, C** · A transliterated instrument name followed by its Latin gloss.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент о шестнадцати струнах | the hekkaidekachord, that is, the instrument of sixteen strings |
| C | геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент из шестнадцати струн | the hekkaidekachord, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings |

**B** · A sixteen-string system, then an instrument within an instrument.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | шестнадцатиструнную систему, то есть шестнадцатиструнный инструмент | a sixteen-string system, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings |

**D** · A sixteen-stringed instrument followed by a restatement of the same number.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | шестнадцатиструнный [инструмент], то есть инструмент с шестнадцатью струнами | the sixteen-stringed instrument, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings |

**Verdict.** Hoc est expressly defines an instrument. The two octachords do furnish a theoretical sixteen-place scheme, but the passage exploits a difference between separately counted and physically present strings. Keeping the name and the immediate gloss makes the wordplay intelligible.

**Russian.** геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент с шестнадцатью струнами  
**English.** the hekkaidekachord, that is, the sixteen-string instrument

Basel 1556, 41.25–42.7 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein p. 207, counting mese twice [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**B · both · minor addition** · confirmed  
“шестнадцатиструнную систему / a sixteen-string system”  
System is an interpretation absent from Xylander’s own gloss instrumentum.  
**Correction.** Retain the name, explain the count in the note.

**C · notes · minor number** · modified  
“1:8 = 8:15”  
In context the colon is intended to link note positions, but the bare equality reads as an equality of numbers, which fails by cross-multiplication. The underlying octave spans are correct.  
**Correction.** Write ‘note 1 is to note 8 as note 8 is to note 15’ or give the two equal octave frequency ratios.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · open** · modified · editorial decision  
**Printed.** perbolæon. Per has sonorum species heccæde / cachordum, hoc est, sedecim chordarum instru- / mentum in quindecim fidibus instructo organo / (Pentecædecachordum uocant Græci) absol- / uitur, fine octachordi ad principium sequentium / continuò assumpto. Quam enim rationem ha-  
**Proposal.** ‘taken in continuity to the beginning of the following [strings]’  
Sequentium is plural and can refer to the ensuing notes or strings. The grave accent in the page image favors adverbial continuo. The shared endpoint makes continuity of succession pertinent.  
Basel 1556, 41.25–42.7 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein p. 207, counting mese twice [checked]

**B · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** perbolæon. Per has sonorum species heccæde / cachordum, hoc est, sedecim chordarum instru- / mentum in quindecim fidibus instructo organo / (Pentecædecachordum uocant Græci) absol- / uitur, fine octachordi ad principium sequentium / continuò assumpto. Quam enim rationem ha-  
**Proposal.** конец октахорда сразу принимают за начало следующих [октахордов] / the end of the octachord being taken immediately as the beginning of the [octachords] that follow  
Only one ensuing octachord is at stake in a two-octave system. The proposed plural ‘octachords’ has no support in the count; a series of notes does.  
Basel 1556, 41.25–42.7 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.20, Friedlein p. 207, counting mese twice [checked]

**C · doubt · unsound** · added  
**Printed.** Per has sonorum species  
**Proposal.** «посредством этих видов звуков» / 'through these species of notes'; «посредством этих звуков» / 'by means of these notes'.  
Omitting species erases Xylander's explicit classification after the enumeration of named notes. ‘These kinds of notes’ keeps both words.  
Xyl. 41.25–42.5 (Basel 1556, page image and printed lines) [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Two octachords yield sixteen counted places and fifteen distinct strings, since the eighth note ends the first and begins the second. Keep this explanation apart from the erroneous numerical equality in the check.

### X16 · Xyl. 42.5–7

*Quam enim rationem habet primus sonus ad octavum, eandem octavus ad decimumquintum obtinet.*

**Sense.** The first note stands to the eighth in the same ratio as the eighth to the fifteenth.

**Russian base · D.** The two parallel relations read naturally and occur in the order of the Latin.

**English base · D.** “Stands in the same ratio” relates the notes rather than their position numbers.

#### Corrections

**A · notes · minor number** · confirmed  
“1st : 8th = octave, 2:1”  
The octave magnitude is correct, but the explicitly ordered ratio needs a quantity: first-to-eighth string lengths give 2:1, frequencies 1:2.  
**Correction.** Distinguish string lengths, ordered frequencies, and the ascending interval multiplier.

**C · notes · minor number** · confirmed  
“1 : 15 = double octave, 4:1”  
The numerals identify scale positions, and the high-to-low frequency ratio or low-to-high length ratio is 4:1. As written the equality looks arithmetical.  
**Correction.** Write ‘the first and fifteenth notes span a double octave’ and specify the orientation of any ratio.

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Both spans encompass seven steps. Their ascending multipliers are 2 and 2, whereas frequencies ordered first-to-eighth and eighth-to-fifteenth give 1:2 each.

### X17 · Xyl. 42.7

*Et haec de sonis.*

**Sense.** This concludes the account of notes.

**Russian base · C = D.** Both use the same idiomatic formula for closure.

**English base · B = C = D.** The three concise wordings are identical and match the transition.

#### Corrections

**A · ru · minor language** · confirmed  
“Это о звуках.”  
An abrupt demonstrative fragment fails to signal closure idiomatically.  
**Correction.** «Вот и всё о звуках.»

### X18 · Xyl. 42.8–15

*Intervallorum autem minimum genus est, quod diesin vocant: deinde semitonium, cuius dupla ad acutum est accessio, respectu dieseos. Deinde est tonus duplici constans semitonio, et rationem sesquioctavam constituens, ut est novenarii ad octonarium numerum. Quo fit, ut semitonium sit quasi dimidium sesquioctavae diesis autem quasi quadrans eiusdem.*

**Sense.** The diesis is the least interval. The semitone rises twice as far as the diesis, and the tone consists of two semitones in the sense explained here. The tone is sesquioctave, nine to eight. The semitone is as it were half, the diesis as it were a quarter, of that interval.

**Russian base · D.** The upward movement and both occurrences of quasi and of the 9:8 ratio are clear.

**English base · D.** Gives an unambiguous rise in pitch and distinguishes both qualified subdivisions.

#### Corrections

**B · en · minor omission** · confirmed  
“and the diesis a quarter of it”  
The text repeats quasi before quadrans. Coordination can carry the earlier qualifier across, but the explicit second qualification disappears.  
**Correction.** “and the diesis, as it were, a quarter of it.”

**C · notes · minor note-claim** · added  
“geometrically, as X19–X20 require”  
The texts give the possibility of a geometrical division of a material magnitude. They do not specify a construction by mean proportionals. Equal pitch-interval divisions have the stated radical ratios, but the derivation should be conditional.  
**Correction.** If the intervals are bisected into equal pitch spans, the factors are the stated square and fourth roots.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · text issue · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** dimidium sesquioctauæ diesis autem (42.14)  
**Proposal.** punctuate: dimidium sesquioctavae, diesis autem  
The page reads sesquioctavae diesis autem without punctuation. Diesis is the new nominative, and autem articulates the second limb; a comma makes the transmitted syntax immediately clear.  
Basel 1556, 42.13–15, scan [checked]

**A · doubt · sound** · added  
**Printed.** dupla ad acutum est accessio, respectu dieseos  
**Proposal.** whose increase toward the high is double  
Accessio can describe approach or increase. Ad acutum sets its direction, and dupla measures how far the pitch rises relative to the diesis. Either sense works if it describes the size of the upward step.  
Xylander (1556), p. 42 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. accessio I and II.B [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The doubling concerns interval size. Equal musical halves and quarters of a 9:8 tone would have ratios √(9/8) and ⁴√(9/8). Preserve both instances of quasi. The passage supplies no integer ratios for its subdivisions.

#### For the editor

- How far should the commentary identify an exact geometric division? Xylander asserts geometric divisibility but supplies no numerical mean here.

### X19 · Xyl. 42.15–18

*Quamvis enim ratione numeri unitas dividi non potest, utique tamen unitas quae in materia conspicitur, dividitur Geometrice.*

**Sense.** The unit is indivisible qua number but divisible geometrically as embodied in matter.

**Russian base · D.** Clearly distinguishes the unit considered as a number from one embodied in matter.

**English base · D.** Preserves the two aspects of unitas, as well as the emphasis of utique.

#### Corrections

**A · notes · minor number** · modified  
“halving the ratio in numbers gives 16 : 17 : 18”  
The check itself recognizes that 17:16 and 18:17 differ. Its opening calls the insertion of an arithmetic mean ‘halving the ratio’, although this halves the difference of the terms. Dividing a numerical unit into rational parts would still not produce the geometric mean.  
**Correction.** Call 17 the arithmetic mean of 16 and 18. The two ratios 17:16 and 18:17 are unequal. An exact midpoint in pitch requires √(16×18).

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · Boethius I.6 distinguishes the lower limit of discrete quantity, the unit, from continuous magnitude such as a divisible line.

### X20 · Xyl. 42.18–21

*Nam chordam eam quae observat rationem sesquioctavam, possibile est dividi in semisesquioctavam, quod est semitonium, et hoc denuo dividi in diesin.*

**Sense.** The string exhibiting the sesquioctave ratio can be divided to obtain a semisesquioctave, a semitone. This in turn can be divided into a diesis.

**Russian base · D.** “Так, чтобы получилось” makes clear what the division yields and avoids the calque «разделить на полутон».

**English base · B = D.** Both express the result of each division and preserve Xylander’s coinage and the ratio bracket.

#### 1. Does the phrase enumerate pieces of the divided string or the interval each division yields?

*dividi in semisesquioctavam … et hoc denuo dividi in diesin*

**A, C** · Retain the literal ‘divide into’ with a singular endpoint.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | разделить на полусверхосминное | divided into the semisesquioctave |
| C | разделить на полусверхосминное | divided into the semisesquioctave |

**B, D** · Make the successive results explicit, B using ‘до’ in Russian.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | разделить до полусверхосминного | divided to yield the semisesquioctave |
| D | разделить так, чтобы получилось | divided to yield a semisesquioctave |

**Verdict.** Hoc resumes the semitone, so the second division operates on the first result. A resultative paraphrase avoids suggesting that physical pieces are each explicitly enumerated. X25 repeats the reasoning.

**Russian.** разделить так, чтобы получился полутон, а при дальнейшем делении — диеса  
**English.** divide to yield a semitone, and divide this in turn to yield a diesis

Basel 1556, 42.18–21 and 43.15–16 [checked] · high confidence · added

#### Corrections

**C · notes · minor note-claim** · added  
“if taken geometrically, as X19 requires”  
A division in geometry need not be a proportional bisection. X19 does not specify the mean proportional. The stated result follows if equal musical steps are assumed.  
**Correction.** if the interval is divided into equal musical steps

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The intermediate length between 8 and 9 is √72, giving equal ratios on each side. Simply bisecting one string yields an octave against the full length.
- **B** · The text itself glosses semisesquioctava as semitonium and gives no separate pair of integers.

### X21 · Xyl. 42.21–25

*Geometriae enim est, semper a maiori recta minorem posse detrahere, semperque posse minori apponere maiorem. ex quo sequitur, ut quaelibet figura ad placitum possit cum augeri, tum diminui.*

**Sense.** Geometry permits a smaller straight line always to be subtracted from a larger, and a larger to be added to a smaller. Any figure can consequently be enlarged and diminished at will.

**Russian base · D.** Отрезок identifies what can be added and subtracted, a finite length of straight line.

**English base · D.** ‘Shorter’ and ‘longer’ precisely express the two measured magnitudes.

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Euclid I.3 gives the corresponding construction: cut from the greater straight line a segment equal to the lesser.

### X22 · Xyl. 42.25–43.1

*Id vero in Arithmetica locum non habet.*

**Sense.** This does not hold in arithmetic.

**Russian base · D.** The idiom denies the applicability of the preceding general claim.

**English base · D.** “This does not hold” sets up the counterexample in arithmetic.

### X23 · Xyl. 43.1–10

*Ad haec, propositum quadratum in aequalia partiri quadrata, aut duplum efficere non potest. Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus, neque in duo potest diducere quadrata (octo enim Arithmetice quadratum esse, aut eius numeri dare radicem quadratam, impossibile est) neque duplum eius aliud quadratum dare (fieri enim non potest, ut numerus triginta duo sit quadratus) At Geometriae hoc factu non est difficile.*

**Sense.** Arithmetic cannot in general divide a given square into equal squares or produce its double as a square. Sixteen cannot be divided into two equal squares, since eight is no square and has no arithmetic square root, and its double thirty-two is no square either. Geometry can do this readily.

**Russian base · B.** The bracketed «равных» makes the argument about bisection clear, although «из чисел» would better preserve numerorum.

**English base · A.** Preserves the printed genitive as ‘among numbers’, the agent carried over from X22 and both arithmetic examples.

#### 1. How does the printed genitive qualify sixteen?

*Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus*

**A** · Partitive: among numbers, sixteen.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Ведь среди чисел шестнадцать | For among numbers sixteen |

**B, C** · The number sixteen, as if numerum or with the partitive left implicit.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Ведь число шестнадцать | the number sixteen |
| C | Ведь число шестнадцать | the number sixteen |

**D** · Introduces sixteen as an example, explaining the genitive in a note.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Таково, например, квадратное число шестнадцать. | Take the number sixteen, which is a square. |

**Verdict.** Numerorum can set the class from which the example is selected. Sedecim can serve as object of diducere, with singular numerus understood for qui. The proposed numerum regularizes the phrase but the print is intelligible.

**Russian.** Ведь из чисел шестнадцать, квадратное число, …  
**English.** For among numbers, sixteen, a square, …

Basel 1556, 43.1–10 and marginal diagrams, scan [checked] · Allen and Greenough §346 [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**D · both · minor sign** · confirmed  
“Кроме того, арифметика / Moreover, arithmetic”  
The subject comes from the preceding segment and is supplied explicitly here. A and C mark the supplement.  
**Correction.** «[арифметика]» / “[arithmetic]”, or use an impersonal construction.

**D · both · minor sign** · added  
“Таково, например, квадратное число шестнадцать. / Take the number sixteen, which is a square.”  
Introducing a new command or separate example sentence adds ‘take/for example’. The bare numeral and the partitive Numerorum carry the example without an imperative.  
**Correction.** Среди чисел шестнадцать — квадратное число / For among numbers, sixteen is a square …

### X24 · Xyl. 43.10–14

*nam Geometer dati quadrati latere loco diametri accepto, ubi circumposuit quadratum, id statim propositi est dimidium: iterumque accepta dati quadrati diametro loco lateris, ex eo quadratum describet, quod propositum dupli esse constat.*

**Sense.** A square built with the given square’s side as its diagonal has half the given area. With the given diagonal as its side it has twice that area. The Latin endings of the last comparison are difficult.

**Russian base · D.** Describes each construction lucidly and attempts to represent propositum dupli as the proposed doubling.

**English base · D.** Makes the geometric result clear while allowing the transmitted word order a construal.

#### 1. Do propositum and dupli describe the original square, the new square or the proposed doubling?

*quod propositum dupli esse constat*

**A, B** · Double of the proposed square: effectively the conjecture propositi duplum.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | вдвое больше предложенного | the double of the one proposed |
| B | вдвое больше данного | twice the size of the original |

**C** · The proposed square at double size.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | есть предложенный [квадрат] в двойном размере | the proposed [square] of double [size] |

**D** · The doubling proposed, or the proposed result of doubling.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | получит искомое удвоение | gives the doubling proposed |

**Verdict.** The side/diagonal construction determines the new area unequivocally. The final case endings do not say ‘the double of the original’ in the ordinary syntax of the immediately preceding propositi … dimidium. Reading propositum substantivally as the result proposed, specified by dupli, is possible but compressed. Print the received comparison and explain the conjecture.

**Russian.** квадрат, который, как видно, даёт искомое удвоение  
**English.** a square which evidently gives the doubling proposed

Basel 1556, 43.10–14, scan [checked] · Euclid, Elements I.47 [checked] · Allen and Greenough §352a on dupli in a specialized genitive of value [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

**A · both · minor construal** · modified  
“вдвое больше предложенного / the double of the one proposed”  
A's doubt acknowledges that this wording assumes propositi duplum. The conjecture is discussed separately, but the running translation gives it priority over the transmitted case endings.  
**Correction.** Represent the received ‘proposed doubling’ and put the smoother construction in the note.

**B · both · minor construal** · modified  
“вдвое больше данного / twice the size of the original”  
The wording makes the direction of comparison explicit from the geometry and is compatible with an interpretive paraphrase. It does not convey the unusual propositum dupli or tell the reader in the running text that the grammatical relation is uncertain.  
**Correction.** Represent the received ‘proposed doubling’ and put the smoother construction in the note.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · By Euclid I.47 the square on the diagonal equals the sum of two squares on the side. Using the old side as a diagonal reverses the operation. With original area 16 the resulting areas are 8 and 32.

#### For the editor

- Should the edition print propositi duplum as a conjecture? The correct area does not determine how the transmitted cases arose.

### X25 · Xyl. 43.15–16

*Sic etiam sesquioctava toni proportio dividi poterit in semitonium, iterumque in diesin.*

**Sense.** Similarly the sesquioctave proportion of the tone can be divided into a semitone and, in turn, into a diesis.

**Russian base · D.** The future and the successive results of division are explicit.

**English base · B.** Retains proportio from the opening terminology and makes each division intelligible.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · For equal interval divisions the square of the half-step ratio gives 9:8, and the square of the quarter-step ratio gives the half-step ratio.

### X26 · Xyl. 43.16–21

*Eorundem quoque auctione, compositae quoque reliquae intervallorum species constituuntur: tridiesis, trisemitonium, ditonus, tritonus, et quo quis modo voluerit, idque rhythmus admiserit, veluti ex sequentibus cognoscetur.*

**Sense.** Increasing these same intervals produces the remaining composite kinds, including tridiesis, trihemitone, ditone, tritone, and such others as one may wish and rhythm allows. The subsequent account illustrates this.

**Russian base · A.** Names the four intervals as prescribed and brackets the understood ‘others’.

**English base · D.** The construction identifies the operation, its products and rhythm as the condition.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**B · text issue · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** compositæ quoque reliquæ inter- / uallorum species

The print clearly reads compositae. X27 explains with autem how intervals formed of smaller magnitudes can be described as incomposite when sounded as one step. Replacing compositae erases the distinction which the next sentence develops.

Keeping compositae preserves the printed wording and allows autem to introduce the distinction between formation and sounding. No change is required.

Incomposita autem

### X27 · Xyl. 43.21–44.1

*Incomposita autem dicuntur, quia tanquam una cantilena ex coalescentibus in unum tribus diesibus, aut semitoniis totidem, aut diversis tonis conflatur, potentia quidem sive facultate diversa intervalla continens, sed unico tamen intervallo edita.*

**Sense.** They are called incomposite because three dieses, as many semitones, or different tones coalesce into what is like a single melodic utterance: it contains different intervals potentially but is uttered as a single interval.

**Russian base · D.** Distinguishes the potentially present smaller intervals from the single interval heard.

**English base · B.** The two short sentences keep the grammatical subject and the potential/actual distinction clear.

#### 1. Does diversis describe qualitative difference or a plurality of tones?

*aut diversis tonis*

**C** · Several tones.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | из нескольких тонов | from several tones |

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |

**B, D** · Different or distinct tones.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | разных тонов | different tones |
| D | из разных тонов | distinct tones |

**Verdict.** The ditone and tritone just enumerated illustrate the joining of more than one tone. The Latin need not posit tones of different size or kind. ‘Several’ expresses the plurality without obscuring the contrast with a single interval.

**English.** or from several tones

“a double rendering of one word”  
Sive shows that the Latin gives a gloss or near-equivalent. It cannot establish how many words stood in an underlying text.  
**Correction.** Call potentia and facultate alternative Latin expressions for potential capacity.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · sound** · modified  
**Printed.** sequentibus cognoscetur. Incomposita autem / dicuntur, quia tanquam una cantilena ex coale- / scentibus in unum tribus diesibus, aut semito- / nijs totidem, aut diuersis tonis conflatur, po- / tentia quidem siue facultate diuersa interualla / continens, sed unico tamen interuallo edita.  
**Proposal.** ‘various (several) tones’ / ‘separate tones’  
‘Several’ works for the magnitudes enumerated in X26. ‘Different’ is possible as long as the reader does not infer that the components have different ratios.

**A · doubt · unsound** · modified

Incomposita autem / dicuntur, quia tanquam una cantilena ex coale- / scentibus in unum tribus diesibus, aut semito- / nijs totidem, aut diuersis tonis conflatur, po- / tentia quidem siue facultate diuersa interualla / continens, sed unico tamen interuallo edita.

Calling cantilena a single sung sound collapses the distinction between one melodic utterance and one note of fixed pitch. X27 specifically says that its sounding spans an interval.

### X28 · Xyl. 44.2–5

*Praeter haec sunt et alia intervalla: diatessaron, quartam vulgo dicunt: diapente, iisdem quinta est: et diapason, quam trito vocabulo octavam nominant.*

**Sense.** There are also diatessaron, diapente and diapason, commonly called fourth, fifth and octave.

**Russian base · C.** Preserves the three pairs of historical names and familiar terms, with their literal senses available in the note to X29.

**English base · D.** Gives each literal sense and its familiar musical name in readable English.

#### 1. How can two series of names remain distinguishable?

*diatessaron, quartam vulgo dicunt: diapente, iisdem quinta est: et diapason … octavam*

**A** · Literal senses of the Greek names paired with the familiar musical terms.

| --- | --- | --- |

The fourth and fifth go by those names in ordinary speech, and the octave is commonly called the “eighth.” |

**C** · Transliterates the Greek names and follows each with its modern musical equivalent.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Кроме них, есть и другие интервалы: диатессарон — в обиходе его называют квартой; диапенте — у тех же это квинта; и диапазон, который расхожим словом именуют октавой. | Besides these there are also other intervals: diatessaron, which is commonly called the fourth; diapente, which for the same people is the fifth; and diapason, which by a well-worn word they name the octave. |

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Помимо этих интервалов есть и другие. Кварту [«через четыре»] в обиходе называют «четвёртой», квинту [«через пять»] те же люди называют «пятой», а октаву [«через все»], по привычному выражению, — «восьмой». | There are other intervals besides these. The interval ‘through four’ is commonly called the fourth, ‘through five’ is what the same people call the fifth, and ‘through all’ goes by the familiar name of octave. |

**Verdict.** The passage exhibits three pairs, including the people designated by iisdem. Either a transliteration or an etymological gloss can keep these distinctions audible. The project specifically permits explaining the names. B's English loses both of the first two pairings.

**Russian.** «Через четыре» в обиходе называют квартой, «через пять» у тех же людей — квинтой, а «через все» привычным словом называют октавой.  
**English.** ‘Through four’ is commonly called the fourth, ‘through five’ the same people call the fifth, and ‘through all’ they know by the familiar name of octave.

### X29 · Xyl. 44.5–13

*Haec autem neque immediata sunt, neque incomposita, sed per media omnia dicta intervalla transeunt. Atque haec quidem intervalla dicuntur, ratione sonitus qui est a primo sono ad extremum: quantitate autem intermediorum sonorum huiusmodi appellationes invenerunt, diatessaron quidem, quia per quatuor sonos aut nervos transit: diapente, quia per quinque: diapason, quia per omnes, hoc est, per octo.*

**Sense.** These are neither immediate nor incomposite. They traverse the intervening intervals described. Their status as intervals refers to the sounding span between the outer notes. Their names refer to the number of notes they traverse: four strings or notes, five, or all eight.

**Russian base · A.** Marks the explanatory additions and relates each modern technical term to its literal sense.

**English base · B.** Distinguishes what defines the interval from what gives each interval its particular name.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · modified  
**Printed.** minant. Hæc autem neque immediata sunt, neque / incomposita, sed per media omnia dicta interual- / la transeunt. Atque hæc quidem interualla dicun- / tur, ratione sonitus qui est à primo sono ad extre / mum: quantitate autem intermediorum sonorum / huiusmodi appellationes inuenerunt, diatessaron / quidem, quia per quatuor sonos aut neruos tran- / sit: diapente, quia per quinque: diapason, quia per / omnes, hoc est, per octo. Nam quia omnia inter-  
**Proposal.** ‘pass through the midst of all the aforesaid intervals’  
Media agrees with intervalla; the traversal concerns the earlier intervals encountered between the extremes. Passing through the middle of each intervening interval would miss the contrast with immediate intervals.  
Lewis & Short, s.v. medius [checked] · Basel 1556, 44.5–13 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Four, five and eight count both bounding notes, although the text calls the notes ‘intervening’. The corresponding step counts are three, four and seven. Sonitus denotes the sounding span here.

#### For the editor

- Should sonitus here be rendered as sounding or sound? The expression concerns the change from the first or low note to the last or high one. The project prescribes note when the word designates an individual musical note.

### X30 · Xyl. 44.13–20

*Nam quia omnia intervalla complentur in octachordo, atque inde iterum geminantur, diapason dicitur, quod per octo nervos transit. Argumento sunt etiam nomina, quae sequentibus intervallis obtigerunt, ut sunt diapason cum diatessaron, diapason cum diapente: disdiapason, hoc est, octava cum quarta, octava cum quinta, et duae octavae.*

**Sense.** All the intervals reach completion in the octachord and are doubled or recur from there. The interval traversing eight strings is therefore called diapason. The names of the larger intervals confirm this explanation: the octave with a fourth, the octave with a fifth, and the double octave, glossed as an eighth with a fourth, an eighth with a fifth, and two octaves.

**Russian base · D.** Keeps the interval names and distinguishes the ordinal glosses. Replace the ambiguous final «две восьмых» with «две октавы».

**English base · D.** Keeps the named compounds and the shift to ordinals. Read the last gloss as two octaves.

#### 1. What recurs beyond the octachord?

*inde iterum geminantur*

**A, C, D** · Are doubled again.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Ведь поскольку все интервалы завершаются в октахорде, а оттуда вновь удваиваются, [интервал] называется октавой [«через все»], потому что проходит через восемь струн. | For because all the intervals are completed within the octachord, and from there are doubled over again, it is called the octave [‘through all’], because it passes through eight strings. |
| C | Ибо, так как все интервалы завершаются в октахорде и оттуда снова удваиваются, [этот интервал] называется диапазоном, поскольку проходит через восемь струн. | For since all the intervals are completed in the octachord and from there are doubled again, it is called diapason, because it passes through eight strings. |
| D | Ведь все интервалы укладываются в октахорд, а затем удваиваются вновь. | For all the intervals are completed within the octachord and thereafter doubled in turn. |

**B** · Are repeated.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Поскольку все интервалы исчерпываются в октахорде, а затем повторяются, октава называется «через все», так как проходит через восемь струн. | For all the intervals are comprised in the octachord and from there are repeated. |

**Verdict.** The compounds illustrate a fresh traversal of positions in the next octave. Geminare can express repetition or doubling. Repetition avoids suggesting that every interval is itself multiplied in size.

**Russian.** а далее повторяются вновь  
**English.** and recur from that point onward

### X31 · Xyl. 44.21–45.2

*Consonant autem diatessaron dictum intervallum, et diapente, secundum id genus consonantiae quod Paraphonum Graeci dicunt, quasi iuxta sonans. Diapason autem, diapason cum diatessaron, diapason cum diapente, et disdiapason, secundum id genus consonantiae quod Antiphonum appellant, quasi contra sonans.*

**Sense.** The fourth and fifth are consonant in the manner called paraphonum by the Greeks, as if sounding beside. The octave, octave and a fourth, octave and a fifth, and double octave belong to the kind called antiphonum, as if sounding opposite.

**Russian base · D.** The two types and their literal glosses are easy to follow.

**English base · A.** The first clause keeps dictum singular, the second makes the supplied verb explicit.

#### 1. Does dictum name the interval or recall an earlier mention?

*diatessaron dictum intervallum*

**A** · The singular interval called the fourth; the fifth is coordinated separately.
