| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Консонируют же названный интервал кварты и квинта — по тому роду консонанса, который греки называют парафонным, как бы «звучащим рядом»; а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому роду консонанса, который называют антифонным, как бы «звучащим напротив». | The aforesaid interval of the fourth, and the fifth, are consonant according to that genus of consonance which the Greeks call paraphonon, as it were ‘sounding beside’; but the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth and the double octave according to that genus of consonance which they call antiphonon, as it were ‘sounding opposite’. |

**Verdict.** The adjacent technical name is best taken as predicative with dictum, as in X28. Reference back to X29 is possible. Strictly, the singular participle modifies only intervallum.

**Russian.** интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта  
**English.** the interval called the fourth, and the fifth

Lewis and Short, s.v. dico [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 44.21–45.2 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · medium confidence · modified

#### 2. Is the gloss spatial opposition or a response?

*quasi contra sonans*

**A, C, D** · Sounding against or opposite.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому роду консонанса, который именуют антифонным, как бы «звучащим против». | while the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave [are consonant] according to that kind of consonance which they call antiphonon, as it were ‘sounding against’. |
| C | а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому роду консонанса, который называют антифонным, как бы «звучащим напротив». | but the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth and the double octave according to that genus of consonance which they call antiphonon, as it were ‘sounding opposite’. |
| D | Октава же, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому виду консонанса, который они называют антифонией, то есть как бы «звучанием напротив». | The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave are consonant in the kind they call antiphony, meaning something like ‘sounding opposite’. |

**B** · English explicitly makes the second sound an answer. Russian has a movement or facing towards.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Октава же, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — согласно тому виду консонанса, который называют антифонией, то есть как бы звучанием навстречу. | The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave are consonant according to the kind they call antiphony, as if sounding in answer. |

**Verdict.** The pairing iuxta/contra is spatial in form. A response is a possible application but the fresh derivation should remain audible, particularly since simultaneity is essential at X32.

**Russian.** как бы «звучащим напротив»  
**English.** as if sounding opposite

Lewis and Short, s.v. contra [from memory] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 44.21–45.2 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**B · en · minor construal** · added  
“as if sounding in answer”  
Contra glosses the direction opposite to iuxta. An answer can imply a later entry, whereas X32 expressly makes these notes simultaneous.  
**Correction.** “as if sounding opposite”

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**C · doubt · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** diatessaron dictum intervallum  
**Proposal.** Консонируют же интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта… / The interval called the fourth and the fifth are consonant…  
Diatessaron supplies the name with which the singular interval is designated. This is the more natural reading here.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 44.21–45.2 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. dico [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · In Boethius’ report of Ptolemy, the octave and double octave are equal-sounding, while the octave compounded with a fourth or fifth belongs to a different class. Compare De institutione musica V.11–12, ed. Friedlein [checked].

### X32 · Xyl. 45.2–13

*Inter paraphonum et antiphonum, hoc est assonans et obsonans, hoc differt, quod illud quidem consonat inaequali tempore, quando soni mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam leniter et concinne sibi invicem succedunt. Antiphonum autem est, cum simul eodem tempore acutum cum gravi consonat: ut cum octavus primo, undecimus quarto, duodecimus quinto, decimusquintus octavo aequaliter ascendit vel descendit in intensionibus remissionibusve, ita ut acutis graves, vel contra, respondeant proportionaliter.*

**Sense.** Paraphony and antiphony, glossed as sounding alongside and sounding opposite, differ in time. In the former the notes succeed one another smoothly and fittingly, with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios. In the latter high and low sound together. The eighth with the first, eleventh with fourth, twelfth with fifth and fifteenth with eighth ascend or descend alike as pitch is raised or lowered, low notes answering high proportionally or conversely.

**Russian base · D.** «В разное время» and the four pairs make the contrast audible.

**English base · D.** Preserves simultaneous parallel changes of pitch and distinguishes them from successive notes.

#### 1. What governs the accusatives?

*mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes … sibi invicem succedunt*

**A** · Supplies the participle ‘keeping’ in brackets.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Между парафонным и антифонным, то есть «звучащим при» и «звучащим навстречу», разница та, что первое консонирует в неравное время, когда звуки, [храня] взаимные свои пропорциональности и отношения, с некоторой равномерностью мягко и складно сменяют друг друга. | the former is consonant at unequal time, when the notes, [keeping] their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and neatly with a certain evenness. |

**B, C, D** · Take the proportions as the respect in which the successive motion is regular. B and D recast the relation in Russian.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | При парафонии звуки консонируют в разное время, когда они плавно и стройно следуют друг за другом, соблюдая некоторую равномерность во взаимных пропорциях и отношениях. | In the former the notes are consonant at unequal times, succeeding one another smoothly and fittingly with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios. |
| C | Между парафонным и антифонным, то есть «призвучным» и «противозвучным», та разница, что первое консонирует в неодинаковое время, когда звуки по своим взаимным пропорциональностям и отношениям с некоторой равномерностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга. | the former is consonant at unequal times, when the notes, in their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness. |
| D | соблюдая известную равномерность в своих взаимных пропорциях и отношениях, они плавно и стройно следуют друг за другом. | In the former the notes are consonant at different times, succeeding one another smoothly and harmoniously with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios. |

**Verdict.** Succedunt takes the dative sibi invicem. No second verb is printed. A's bracketed supplement is an intelligible expedient. A construction of respect or an implicit secundum is preferable to declaring a lacuna. The standard Greek accusative examples concern the part affected, so the parallel is suggestive only.

**Russian.** когда звуки плавно и стройно сменяют друг друга с известной равномерностью в своих взаимных пропорциях и отношениях  
**English.** when the notes succeed one another smoothly and harmoniously with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios

Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar §397b, Dickinson College edition [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### 2. Do the glosses refer to accompaniment, response or opposition?

*assonans et obsonans*

**A, C** · Give directional calques (to/against).

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Между парафонным и антифонным, то есть «звучащим при» и «звучащим навстречу», разница та, что первое консонирует в неравное время, когда звуки, [храня] взаимные свои пропорциональности и отношения, с некоторой равномерностью мягко и складно сменяют друг друга. | Between the paraphonon and the antiphonon, that is, the ‘sounding to’ and the ‘sounding over against’, the difference is this: |
| C | Между парафонным и антифонным, то есть «призвучным» и «противозвучным», та разница, что первое консонирует в неодинаковое время, когда звуки по своим взаимным пропорциональностям и отношениям с некоторой равномерностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга. | Between the paraphonon and the antiphonon, that is, the ‘sounding-to’ and the ‘sounding-against’, there is this difference: |

**B** · Calls the second sounding a response and the first an accompaniment or echo.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Парафония и антифония, то есть созвучие вторящее и ответное, различаются следующим. | Paraphony and antiphony, that is, sounding in accompaniment and sounding in answer, differ as follows. |

**D** · Has answering sound first and opposite sound second.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Парафония и антифония, то есть «звучание в ответ» и «встречное звучание», различаются следующим образом. | Paraphony and antiphony, that is, ‘sounding in response’ and ‘sounding opposite’, differ in this respect. |

**Verdict.** X31 supplies the author's own near equivalents, iuxta and contra. The glosses should distinguish the two directions. Rendering either as answering is less precise and can wrongly suggest that the second sound follows in time.

**Russian.** «звучащее рядом» и «звучащее напротив»  
**English.** ‘sounding alongside’ and ‘sounding opposite’

Lewis and Short, s.v. assono and obsono [checked] · Xylander X31–32 [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**A · both · minor language** · confirmed  
“«звучащим при» / at unequal time”  
The isolated Russian preposition needs a complement. The singular English time is unidiomatic in the temporal contrast.  
**Correction.** «звучащим рядом» / at different times

**C · notes · minor note-claim** · confirmed  
“is an accusative of respect without a preposition”  
The proposed syntax is possible but §397b concerns the part affected. The categorical claim exceeds that evidence.  
**Correction.** Present the construction of respect as a possibility and mention the implicit-preposition analysis.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · text issue · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** quando soni mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam leniter et concinne sibi invicem succedunt  
**Proposal.** no change proposed; possibly a participle (servantes, retinentes) has dropped out after rationes  
The scan has the two accusatives and no participle. The dative supplies the complement of succedunt. Keeping or following makes good sense as a supplement for translation, but there is no material evidence of lost letters.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar §397b, Dickinson College edition [checked]

**A · doubt · sound** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** quando soni mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam leniter et concinne sibi invicem succedunt  
**Proposal.** когда звуки в отношении взаимных своих пропорциональностей и отношений с некоторой ровностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга / when the notes, in respect of their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness  
This interprets the awkward case without changing the print. The classical construction is only an analogy.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar §397b, Dickinson College edition [checked]

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** inaequali tempore  
**Proposal.** in unequal durations  
Unequal durations are not required by the order of entry. The explicit antithesis is with simultaneous sounding.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** undecimus quarto, duodecimus quinto, decimusquintus octavo  
**Proposal.** the eleventh, twelfth and fifteenth [notes] against the first  
The four pairs all differ by seven positions. Their being octave examples does not oblige Xylander to illustrate every case in X31. Altering the lower notes would replace his examples.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** aequalitate quadam... aequaliter  
**Proposal.** с неким равенством … равно восходит или нисходит / with a certain equality... rises or falls equally  
Aequalitas allows equality and evenness. Parallel motion makes the equality precise in the second instance. For the first, evenness is less abstract.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. aequalitas [checked]

**B · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** inaequali tempore  
**Proposal.** При парафонии звуки консонируют в течение неодинаковых промежутков времени. / In paraphony the notes are consonant for unequal spans of time.  
Simul eodem tempore contrasts with notes succeeding one another. Unequal durations would add a distinct rhythmic condition.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**C · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** inaequali tempore  
**Proposal.** …первое консонирует в неравное по длительности время… / …the former is consonant in an unequal span of time…  
The contrast with simultaneous production establishes distinct occasions, irrespective of how long each note is held.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**C · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** assonans et obsonans  
**Proposal.** ‘answering’ and ‘counter-sounding’  
The glosses in X31 favour directional language. An answering or counter-sounding interpretation is defensible if no temporal response is implied.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. assono and obsono [checked]

**D · doubt · sound** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes … sibi invicem succedunt  
**Proposal.** звуки плавно и стройно сменяют друг друга, [следуя] своим взаимным пропорциям и отношениям с известной равномерностью / the notes succeed one another smoothly and harmoniously, [following] their mutual proportions and ratios with a certain evenness  
Following is an explicit editorial supplement for the relation between the two accusatives and the succession of notes.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar §397b, Dickinson College edition [checked]

**D · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** inaequali tempore  
**Proposal.** через неодинаковые промежутки времени / at unequal intervals of time  
Different moments are explicit from the opposition to simul. The text gives neither the duration of the notes nor the spacing between successive onsets.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.2–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **A** · Each ordinal pair differs by seven steps. All four examples are octaves, although X31 includes larger compounds in the antiphonic class.

#### For the editor

- Should the translation supply a bracketed participle or leave the relation of the accusatives open in a note?

### X33 · Xyl. 45.13–16

*Caeterum tonus, semitonium, atque diesis per se considerata, initium quidem seu principium sunt consonantiae, non tamen consonantia.*

**Sense.** Tone, semitone and diesis in themselves are the beginning or principle of consonance, yet do not themselves constitute consonance.

**Russian base · A = C.** Identical Russian versions preserve the concessive turn and the paired names for a principle.

**English base · C.** The two near synonyms and the predicative contrast are retained concisely.

#### 1. Does the conclusion say ‘not yet’?

*non tamen consonantia*

**A, C** · Both languages have an adversative contrast.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Впрочем, тон, полутон и диеса, рассматриваемые сами по себе, суть начало, или первооснова, консонанса, но не консонанс. | For the rest, the tone, the semitone and the diesis, considered in themselves, are indeed the beginning, or principle, of consonance, yet not a consonance. |
| C | Впрочем, тон, полутон и диеса, рассматриваемые сами по себе, суть начало, или первооснова, консонанса, но не консонанс. | Tone, semitone and diesis, however, considered in themselves, are the beginning, or principle, of consonance, yet not consonance. |

**B, D** · Russian has ещё, while the English is adversative.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | А тон, полутон и диеса, взятые сами по себе, служат началом, или первоосновой, консонанса, но консонанса ещё не образуют. | The tone, semitone, and diesis, considered in themselves, are the start or beginning of consonance, though they do not constitute consonance. |
| D | Тон, полутон и диеса, взятые сами по себе, составляют начало, или исходную точку, консонанса, но ещё не консонанс. | The tone, the semitone and the diesis, considered in themselves, are the beginning or starting point of consonance, though they are not themselves consonance. |

**Verdict.** Tamen counters quidem: they are the beginnings of consonance but not themselves consonance. Vinet's nondum, ‘not yet’, is a different formulation. The Russian addition implies a stage in a progression.

**Russian.** но сами консонанса не образуют  
**English.** yet they do not themselves constitute consonance

Lewis and Short, s.v. tamen [from memory] · Xylander X33 and Vinet V38 [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**B · ru · minor addition** · confirmed  
“консонанса ещё не образуют”  
Ещё introduces a temporal qualification for tamen.  
**Correction.** но сами консонанса не образуют

**D · ru · minor addition** · confirmed  
“ещё не консонанс”  
Ещё introduces a temporal qualification for tamen.  
**Correction.** но сами консонанса не образуют

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Vinet says ‘not yet’, where Xylander makes a concessive distinction.

### X34 · Xyl. 45.16–24

*Conficitur autem diatessaron duobus ex tonis, unoque, semitonio, et praeterea quopiam exiguo: quod quia minus diesi est, appellationem peculiarem non invenit. Diapente vero ex tribus tonis, semitonioque constat. Diapason autem ex sex tonis conficitur. Diapason autem cum diatessaron, ex octo tonis et semitonio conflatur. Diapason cum diapente, ex novem tonis et semitonio. Disdiapason, ex duodecim tonis.*

**Sense.** A fourth comprises two tones, a semitone and an unnamed quantity smaller than a diesis. A fifth has three tones and a semitone, an octave six tones, an octave and a fourth eight tones and a semitone, an octave and a fifth nine tones and a semitone, and a double octave twelve tones.

**Russian base · B.** «Малая добавка» makes it clear that the unnamed amount is additional.

**English base · B = D.** Both preserve one semitone plus a further positive amount, together with every subsequent total.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · text issue · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** duobus ex tonis, unoqúe semitonio, et præterea quopiam exiguo (45.17: no comma after unoque, in the diplomatic transcription and on the page image)  
**Proposal.** unoque semitonio (no comma), as printed  
The punctuation-like mark belongs to the printed abbreviation of -que. There is no separate comma after it. Uno agrees with semitonio. Removing the extra comma changes no interval.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.16–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**C · text issue · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** duobus ex tonis, unoqúe semitonio, et præterea quo  
**Proposal.** duobus ex tonis, unoque semitonio  
The punctuation-like mark belongs to the printed abbreviation of -que. There is no separate comma after it. Uno agrees with semitonio. Removing the extra comma changes no interval.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.16–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** unoque semitonio, et praeterea quopiam exiguo  
**Proposal.** read the ‘small thing’ as a correction of the semitone instead of an addition  
Et praeterea explicitly adds a small amount. It does not subtract anything from, or qualify the value of, the semitone.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.16–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

**B · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** praeterea quopiam exiguo  
**Proposal.** The unnamed quantity could be a tuning discrepancy.  
The text gives only a size bound. Naming the Pythagorean comma would conflict with the direction of the discrepancy if semitone means an exact half of 9:8.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 45.16–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.3 and I.16–18, ed. Friedlein (1867), TML [checked] · Vitruvius, De architectura V.4.3, LacusCurtius [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · With an exact half of the 9:8 tone, two and a half tones already exceed 4:3 by 11.73 cents. Adding a positive amount cannot give the ratio in X35.
- **C** · The positive remainder is also absent from X47 and from the sum for the octave and a fourth in X34 itself.

#### For the editor

- Should the reading text drop the comma after unoque, as the print and agreement suggest?

### X35 · Xyl. 45.24–46.3

*Proportio autem diatessaron seu quartae est sesquitertia, diapente sesquialteram constituit, diapason duplam. Diapason cum diatessaron, duplam superpartientem: diapason cum diapente, triplam.*

**Sense.** The fourth is sesquitertian, the fifth sesquialter and the octave duple. An octave and a fourth makes a duple superpartient ratio and an octave and a fifth a triple ratio.

**Russian base · D.** All five ratios have the prescribed names and brackets; the doublet remains visible.

**English base · D.** Gives both the literal interval name and the ordinary name, followed by all five correct ratios.

#### 1. How is the explanatory pair represented?

*diatessaron seu quartae*

**A** · Literal etymology followed by the usual term.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Пропорция же интервала «через четыре», или кварты, — сверхтретная [4:3], квинта образует полуторную [3:2], октава — двукратную [2:1]; | The proportion of the ‘through four’, or fourth, is sesquitertian [4:3]; |

**B** · Russian gives the ordinary term and an ordinal gloss. English compresses both names into one.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Пропорция кварты, или «четвёртой», — сверхтретная [4:3]. | The proportion of the interval commonly called the fourth is sesquitertian [4:3]. |

**C** · Transliterates the first name before giving its equivalent.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Пропорция же диатессарона, или кварты, — сверхтретная [4:3]; | The proportion of the diatessaron, or fourth, is sesquitertian [4:3]; |

**D** · Russian uses an ordinal gloss after the usual name. English gives the literal etymology first.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Отношение кварты, или «четвёртой», — сверхтретное [4:3], квинта образует полуторное [3:2], октава — двукратное [2:1]. | The ratio of the interval ‘through four’, or fourth, is sesquitertian [4:3]. |

**Verdict.** Seu introduces an alternative designation. Keep both nomenclatures perceptible, either by an etymological gloss or in a note. All four correctly compute every ratio.

**Russian.** отношение интервала «через четыре», или кварты  
**English.** the ratio of the interval ‘through four’, or fourth

Project conventions §§4.1–4.2 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.4, ed. Friedlein (1867), pp. 191–192, TML [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**B · en · minor omission** · confirmed  
“the interval commonly called the fourth”  
Only one designation appears, although the Latin explicitly pairs diatessaron with quarta. The label ‘commonly’ echoes X28 but is absent here.  
**Correction.** the interval ‘through four’, or fourth

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · Duple superpartient designates a class; the octave combined with the fourth gives its particular value 8:3. Compare X36 and Boethius V.11–12 [checked].

### X36 · Xyl. 46.3–9

*Id quo melius intelligatur, exempli causa constituatur in numeris primus terminus senarius: ad eum sesquitertius est octonarius, sesquialter novenarius, duplus duodenarius, duplus superpartiens (nimirum super bipartiens tertias) sedecim. Triplus denique, octodecim: quadruplus, viginti quatuor.*

**Sense.** Set six as the first numerical term. Eight stands to it as 4:3, nine as 3:2, twelve as 2:1 and sixteen as 8:3, the last exceeding twice six by two thirds of six. Eighteen is triple and twenty-four quadruple.

**Russian base · B.** Explains the two thirds without an awkward compound and keeps the common base six.

**English base · B.** Distinguishes the first term from each relative number and explains the excess over the double.

#### 1. Does the parenthesis name a second ratio?

*duplus superpartiens (nimirum super bipartiens tertias)*

**A, C** · Use a technical compound but give one bracket for the whole ratio.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | сверхтретное [4:3] к ней [число] — восемь, полуторное [3:2] — девять, двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (а именно сверхдвучастное третей) — шестнадцать; | to it the number eight is sesquitertian [4:3], nine sesquialter [3:2], twelve duple [2:1], sixteen duple superpartient [8:3] (that is to say, superbipartient thirds); |
| C | к нему сверхтретным [4:3] является восемь, полуторным [3:2] — девять, двукратным [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратным сверхчастным [8:3] (а именно сверхдвучастным по третям) — шестнадцать, наконец, трёхкратным [3:1] — восемнадцать, четырёхкратным [4:1] — двадцать четыре. | to it the sesquitertian [4:3] is eight, the sesquialter [3:2] nine, the duple [2:1] twelve, the duple superpartient [8:3] (namely superbipartient by thirds) sixteen; |

**B** · Explains the fractional excess in ordinary words.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Сверхтретное [4:3] к нему — восемь, полуторное [3:2] — девять, двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (с избытком именно в две трети) — шестнадцать. | Eight stands to it in a sesquitertian [4:3] ratio, nine in a sesquialter [3:2], twelve in a duple [2:1], sixteen in a duple superpartient [8:3] (with precisely two thirds in excess). |

**D** · Supplies the understood ‘double’ and repeats [8:3].

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | По отношению к нему восемь составляет сверхтретное [4:3], девять — полуторное [3:2], двенадцать — двукратное [2:1], шестнадцать — двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (точнее, [двукратное] с избытком в две трети [8:3]). | Relative to it, eight is sesquitertian [4:3], nine sesquialter [3:2], twelve duple [2:1], and sixteen duple superpartient [8:3] (specifically, [the double] plus two thirds [8:3]). |

**Verdict.** All agree on 16:6 = 8:3. The parenthesis specifies which parts are present in addition to twice six. Standing alone a superbipartient ratio by thirds is 5:3, which would misrepresent this construction. A verbal explanation of the excess removes the question of a second numerical gloss.

**Russian.** двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (с избытком в две трети меньшего члена)  
**English.** duple superpartient [8:3] (with an excess of two thirds of the smaller term)

Boethius, De institutione musica I.4, ed. Friedlein (1867), pp. 191–192, TML [checked] · Project conventions §4.3 [checked] · high confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**C · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** duplus superpartiens (nimirum super bipartiens tertias)  
**Proposal.** Repeat [8:3] after the parenthetical specification.  
A repeated [8:3] can accompany the fully supplied compound expression as D shows. Without the supplied ‘duple’ a standalone bracket would be ambiguous. None of the approaches gives a different numerical result.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.3–9 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.4, ed. Friedlein (1867), pp. 191–192, TML [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **A** · Distinguish the whole ratio 16:6 from the simple superbipartient by thirds, 5:3. Here the two-thirds excess follows two wholes.

#### For the editor

- Should a parenthetic specification of the same named ratio repeat its bracket? The prose can instead explain the extra two thirds.

### X37 · Xyl. 46.9–13

*At enim scire hoc oportet, proportiones intervallorum nequaquam iudicari ad numeros sonorum vel fidium facta collatione, sed ad sonitus ipsius intensionem a gravi ad acutum.*

**Sense.** Interval ratios must be judged by the raising of the sound itself from low to high, rather than by comparing counts of notes or strings.

**Russian base · D.** The following ordinal experiment makes D's description of the numbers particularly apt.

**English base · D.** Makes the distinction between numbered positions and the rise in pitch explicit.

#### 1. Is intensio physical tension or a rise in pitch?

*ad sonitus ipsius intensionem a gravi ad acutum*

**A, C** · Literal tension or strain, glossed as pitch in the commentary.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | о пропорциях интервалов судят отнюдь не путём сопоставления с числами звуков или струн, а по напряжению самого звучания от низкого к высокому. | the proportions of the intervals are by no means judged by making a comparison with the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the tension of the sound itself from low to high. |
| C | Однако надо знать, что пропорции интервалов отнюдь не определяются сопоставлением с числами звуков или струн, но [сопоставлением] с напряжением самого звучания от низкого к высокому. | the proportions of intervals are by no means judged by a comparison made with the numbers of notes or strings, but with the tension of the sounding itself from low to high. |

**B, D** · The rise of the sounding pitch from low to high.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Надо, однако, знать, что пропорции интервалов определяют отнюдь не сопоставлением количества звуков или струн, а по повышению самого звучания от низкого к высокому. | One must, however, bear in mind that the proportions of intervals are assessed by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high. |
| D | Нужно, однако, знать, что об отношениях интервалов судят вовсе не по сопоставлению чисел, обозначающих звуки или струны, а по тому, насколько само звучание повышается от низкого к высокому. | One must understand, however, that the ratios of intervals are in no way judged by a comparison of the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high. |

**Verdict.** The genitive is ‘of the sound itself’; a gravi ad acutum specifies a change in pitch. Tension of a string is a means of producing the change, treated separately under tensio in X41. The physical word by itself gives a misleading description of what is measured.

**Russian.** по повышению самого звучания от низкого к высокому  
**English.** by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high

Boethius, De institutione musica I.3 and I.16–18, ed. Friedlein (1867), TML [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.9–13 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### 2. A comparison with counts or with the numbers assigned to particular notes?

*ad numeros sonorum vel fidium*

**A, C** · Numbers of notes or strings, allowing count or assigned labels.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Однако следует знать вот что: о пропорциях интервалов судят отнюдь не путём сопоставления с числами звуков или струн, а по напряжению самого звучания от низкого к высокому. | But this must be known: the proportions of the intervals are by no means judged by making a comparison with the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the tension of the sound itself from low to high. |
| C | Однако надо знать, что пропорции интервалов отнюдь не определяются сопоставлением с числами звуков или струн, но [сопоставлением] с напряжением самого звучания от низкого к высокому. | But one must know this: the proportions of intervals are by no means judged by a comparison made with the numbers of notes or strings, but with the tension of the sounding itself from low to high. |

**B** · Numbers in English; the collective quantity in Russian.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Надо, однако, знать, что пропорции интервалов определяют отнюдь не сопоставлением количества звуков или струн, а по повышению самого звучания от низкого к высокому. | One must, however, bear in mind that the proportions of intervals are assessed by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high. Comparing the numbers of notes or strings plays no part in that assessment. |

**D** · Explicitly assigned numbers in Russian, with the general plural in English.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Нужно, однако, знать, что об отношениях интервалов судят вовсе не по сопоставлению чисел, обозначающих звуки или струны, а по тому, насколько само звучание повышается от низкого к высокому. | One must understand, however, that the ratios of intervals are in no way judged by a comparison of the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high. |

**Verdict.** X38 compares position five with positions one through four. The reference is to numbers in the ordered series. A literal plural ‘numbers’ is workable with a note; the collective quantity alone obscures the example.

**Russian.** по числам, обозначающим звуки или струны  
**English.** by comparing the position numbers of notes or strings

Xylander X37–38, Basel 1556, p. 46 [checked] · high confidence · added

#### Corrections

**A · both · minor mistranslation** · confirmed  
“напряжению самого звучания / the tension of the sound itself”  
The acoustic sense is present only indirectly. A reader can take this for force or string tension.  
**Correction.** повышение самого звучания / the rise in pitch of the sound itself

**C · both · minor mistranslation** · confirmed  
“напряжением самого звучания / the tension of the sounding itself”  
The acoustic sense is present only indirectly. A reader can take this for force or string tension.  
**Correction.** повышение самого звучания / the rise in pitch of the sound itself

**B · ru · minor construal** · added  
“сопоставлением количества звуков или струн”  
Количество suggests the aggregate count; the demonstration in X38 compares the fifth position with each preceding one.  
**Correction.** «сопоставляя порядковые номера звуков или струн»

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The next segment uses ordinal labels, and it is these labels that fail to yield the pitch ratio.

#### For the editor

- Should sonitus here be rendered as sounding or sound? The expression concerns the change from the first or low note to the last or high one. The project prescribes note when the word designates an individual musical note.

### X38 · Xyl. 46.13–21

*Nam quia intervallum diapente diximus habere sesquialteram proportionem, equidem id non intelligendum est ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet. Ea namque ratione ad primum esset quincuplum, ad secundum duplum sesquialterum: at tertio collatum, eum, eiusque bessem continet: sesquiquartum vero est ad quartum, nulli autem horum omnium sesquialtera proportione quadrat.*

**Sense.** The fifth's sesquialter ratio does not refer to the excess determined from its position relative to earlier notes. By that reckoning it is five times the first and two and a half times the second. It contains the third plus two thirds of it, and stands to the fourth as five to four. None of these comparisons yields three to two.

**Russian base · B.** The bracketed ordinal number clarifies the argument, and every named ratio is marked.

**English base · D.** The bracketed fifth note identifies the referent without committing the reader to a conjectural relative.

#### 1. How is qua construed, and what is se attached to?

*qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet*

**A** · Admits translating as though quam were printed, with se as the object of praecedentium.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Ведь раз мы сказали, что интервал квинты имеет полуторную [3:2] пропорцию, то понимать это, конечно, следует не с оглядкой на то количественное превышение, какое у него есть по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам. | For whereas we said that the interval of the fifth has the sesquialter [3:2] proportion, this is certainly not to be understood with an eye to the excess of quantity which it has with respect to the notes preceding it. |

**C** · Reads qua adverbially and assumes a tacit object for habet.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Ведь когда мы сказали, что интервал квинты имеет полуторную [3:2] пропорцию, это, конечно, не следует понимать, глядя на избыток количества — в той мере, в какой он имеет его по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам. | For since we said that the interval of the fifth has the sesquialter [3:2] proportion, this is certainly not to be understood by looking at the excess of quantity, in so far as it has it in respect of the notes preceding it. |

**B, D** · Paraphrase the numerical comparison without committing to a single analysis of qua.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Когда мы говорим, что у квинты полуторная [3:2] пропорция, речь, конечно, не о том, насколько её [порядковый номер] превосходит номера предшествующих звуков. | For when we said that the fifth has a sesquialter [3:2] proportion, we certainly did not mean this in terms of its numerical excess over the notes that precede it. |
| D | Сказав, что интервал квинты имеет полуторное [3:2] отношение, мы, конечно, не имели в виду, на сколько по количеству [пятый звук] превосходит предшествующие ему звуки. | When we said that the interval of a fifth has a sesquialter [3:2] ratio, we certainly did not mean the numerical excess of [the fifth note] over the notes preceding it. |

**Verdict.** The page clearly has qua. An ablative referring to quantitas with se habet would mean ‘in which it stands in relation to the preceding notes’. Adverbial qua with an understood object is also possible. A translation that explicitly presupposes quam goes beyond the reading text. If restored, feminine quam would refer to quantitas, not the masculine excessus.

**Russian.** по численному превышению над предшествующими звуками  
**English.** by its numerical excess in relation to the preceding notes

Lewis and Short, s.v. qua and habeo [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.13–21 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.4, ed. Friedlein (1867), pp. 191–192, TML [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**A · both · minor construal** · confirmed  
“какое у него есть / which it has”  
A explicitly bases this relative object on conjectural quam. The reading text gives qua and does not express a relative object.  
**Correction.** по численному превышению над предыдущими звуками / by its numerical excess over the preceding notes

**A · notes · major note-claim** · modified  
“excessum (or quantitatem)... habet”  
A relative pronoun taking excessus as antecedent would be masculine quem. Feminine quam can take quantitas as antecedent. The two nouns cannot simply be interchanged in the proposed repair.  
**Correction.** Distinguish quam referring to quantitas from quem referring to excessus.

**C · notes · major note-claim** · modified  
“After excessum quantitatis one expects a relative object, quam … habet ('the excess that it has').”  
The gloss makes the relative an object referring to the excess. If its antecedent is excessus, the object pronoun would be quem. Feminine quam would instead resume quantitas.  
**Correction.** Distinguish quam referring to quantitas from quem referring to excessus.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · text issue · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet  
**Proposal.** perhaps quam... habet  
The scan has qua with no visible nasal stroke. Quam could agree with quantitas, although quem would be required to resume excessus. The ablative with se habet and the adverbial construal offer alternatives without altering the type.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.13–21 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. qua and habeo [checked]

**C · text issue · sound** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** spiciendo, qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum  
**Proposal.** Keep qua (adverbial, 'in so far as'); quam is possible but not necessary.  
Keep the visible qua. Taking it as an adverb or ablative and assigning se accordingly is possible. The following comparisons establish the quantity meant. The precise attachment is open.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.13–21 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. qua and habeo [checked]

**A · doubt · sound** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet  
**Proposal.** the excess of quantity in which it stands with respect to the preceding notes  
Ablative qua referring to quantitas and reflexive se habet are compatible with the printed form. Se may alternatively go with the participle.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.13–21 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. habeo [checked]

The next eum resumes an individual note, and the other three comparisons use ordinals. The grave accent does not establish a new enumeration.

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Bes is two thirds. Five stands to three as the whole plus two thirds. The wording describes this ratio without naming it, so no [5:3] is needed. Boethius I.4 [checked] distinguishes 5:3 from 5:2.

### X39 · Xyl. 46.21–23

*Ergo ad intensionem acuitatis, non ad numerum sonorum respiciendum. eo enim tandem modo sesquialterum erit,*

**Sense.** One must attend to the rise in pitch rather than to the number of notes. Only in that way does the fifth stand in a sesquialter ratio.

**Russian base · D.** The phrasing makes the increase in pitch and the return to the fifth’s ratio clear.

**English base · B = D.** Both give the pitch rise and retain the required [3:2].

#### 1. How are the change in pitch and the restriction of method rendered?

*ad intensionem acuitatis … eo enim tandem modo*

**A** · Tension of high pitch, and only in that way.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Итак, смотреть надо на напряжение высоты, а не на число звуков: | Therefore one must look to the tension of high pitch, not to the number of the notes; |

**C** · Tension of acuteness, with tandem made temporal.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Следовательно, надо смотреть на напряжение высоты, а не на число звуков: | Therefore one must look to the tension of acuteness, not to the number of notes; |

**B, D** · A rise in pitch, and only thus.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Следовательно, надо смотреть на повышение звука, а не на число звуков: | We must therefore consider the rise in pitch instead of the number of notes. |
| D | Следовательно, смотреть нужно на повышение звука, а не на число звуков. | We must therefore look to the rise in pitch, not to the number of notes. |

**Verdict.** X37 defines the relevant motion from low to high. Tandem brings the argument to its conclusion; no temporal event changes the fifth into a sesquialter interval. ‘Only in this way’ is idiomatic here.

**Russian.** надо учитывать повышение звука: лишь тогда [отношение] будет полуторным [3:2]  
**English.** consider the rise in pitch: only thus will the [ratio] be sesquialter [3:2]

Boethius, De institutione musica I.3 and I.16–18, ed. Friedlein (1867), TML [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. tandem [checked] · Xylander X37–39 [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

**A · both · minor language** · confirmed  
“напряжение высоты / the tension of high pitch”  
The phrase suggests physical strain and gives no clear direction to the change in pitch.  
**Correction.** повышение звука / the rise in pitch

**C · both · minor language** · confirmed  
“напряжение высоты / the tension of acuteness”  
The phrase suggests physical strain and gives no clear direction to the change in pitch.  
**Correction.** повышение звука / the rise in pitch

### X40 · Xyl. 46.23–24

*quod idem de reliquis existimandum est.*

**Sense.** The same conclusion applies to the remaining intervals.

**Russian base · B.** The added referent is both clear and bracketed.

**English base · B.** The explicit, bracketed intervals give the correct scope to the continuation.

### X41 · Xyl. 46.24–47.5

*Porro hae proportiones intervallorum, aut per differentiam longitudinis, crassitieive nervorum, aut per tensionem, quae fit conversione clavorum, deprehensae sunt, aut a ponderum tractione forte manifestius: in illis vero quae inflantur, vel per cavitatis amplitudinem, vel ab intensione et remissione spiritus.*

**Sense.** These ratios were discovered through differences in string length or thickness, through tension produced by turning the pegs, or perhaps more clearly through the pull of weights. In wind instruments, the evidence came from the size of the cavity or from increasing and decreasing the breath.

**Russian base · B.** Accurately differentiates string tension from the rise or fall in force of the breath.

**English base · D.** The supplied noun is marked, and the pull of the weights, scope of the cavity and breath remain distinct.

#### 1. Is a particular dimension of the cavity meant?

*per cavitatis amplitudinem*

**A, B, D** · Size or capacity of the cavity.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | а в тех [инструментах], в которые дуют, — либо по величине полости, либо по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания. | and in those [instruments] that are blown, either through the size of the cavity, or from the tension and relaxation of the breath. |
| B | В духовых инструментах их обнаружили по величине полости либо по усилению и ослаблению дыхания. | In instruments that are blown, they were discovered from the size of the cavity or from the strengthening and weakening of the breath. |
| D | Для духовых [инструментов] их установили либо по величине внутренней полости, либо по усилению и ослаблению струи воздуха. | In wind [instruments] they were discovered either from the size of the cavity or from the strengthening and slackening of the breath. |

**C** · Specifically its width.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | а в тех [инструментах], в которые дуют, — либо через ширину полости, либо по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания. | and in those [instruments] that are blown, either through the width of the bore or from the tension and slackening of the breath. |

**Verdict.** Amplitudo allows both breadth and overall extent. A bore’s width is a possible, narrower application, and the print supplies no axis. ‘Size of the cavity’ preserves that openness.

**Russian.** по величине полости  
**English.** from the size of the cavity

Lewis and Short, s.v. amplitudo [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.24–47.5 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · medium confidence · modified

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** a ponderum tractione forte manifestius  
**Proposal.** or, as it happens, more plainly from the pull of weights  
Perhaps marks a tentative comparison of evidential clarity. An occurrence by chance is linguistically possible but adds an unmotivated historical claim.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.24–47.5 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. forte [from memory]

**A · doubt · unsound** · modified  
**Printed.** conversione clavorum  
**Proposal.** turning of the keys  
Turning a clavus here tightens a string. In English ‘keys’ can obscure the peg/key distinction; a tuning key could serve as the tool, but is not the piece whose rotation tensions the string.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 46.24–47.5 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The string law distinguishes a tension ratio from a pitch ratio. At fixed length and linear density an octave requires four times the tension. Boethius I.10–11 [checked] reports the traditional trials.

#### For the editor

- Is there sufficient reason to identify the dimension of the cavity as its width?

### X42 · Xyl. 47.5–17

*Porro systematum aliud est tetrachordum, quod quatuor componitur nervis, omnem vim intervalli diatessaron complectens: aliud pentachordum, quinque ex nervis constitutum, diapente intervalli omnem potestatem in se continens: aliud octachordum, octo ex nervis constructum, omnem octavae materiem ambiens. Est et quod continentibus sedecim nervis conficitur, omnem disdiapason (decimamquintam hodie vocant) intervalli ambitum comprehendens, eoque ad duplices usus habile, quod et praedicta intervalla, et ex his ipsis composita complectitur.*

**Sense.** Among systems, the four-string tetrachord encompasses the full power of the fourth, the five-string pentachord the full capacity of the fifth, and the eight-string octachord all the material of the octave. A further system of sixteen consecutive strings encompasses the whole range of the double octave, now called a fifteenth. It serves two uses because it comprises the foregoing intervals and their compounds.

**Russian base · D.** Preserves sixteen consecutive strings alongside the name квинтдецима and the two uses.

**English base · D.** Keeps the numerical difficulty visible and distinguishes power, capacity, content and compass.

#### 1. Is materies literal matter or the contents of the octave?

*omnem octavae materiem ambiens*

**A, B, C** · Use the literal matter or material.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | третья — октахорд, построенный из восьми струн и объемлющий всю материю октавы. | another the octachord, constructed of eight strings, encompassing the whole matter of the octave. |
| B | Ещё одна — октахорд, построенный из восьми струн и охватывающий всю материю октавы. | Another is the octachord, built from eight strings and encompassing the whole material of the octave. |
| C | ещё одна — октахорд, построенный из восьми струн и объемлющий всю материю октавы. | another the octachord, constructed of eight strings, encompassing the whole matter of the octave. |

**D** · Uses content.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Ещё одна — октахорд, построенный из восьми струн и охватывающий всё содержание октавы. | Another is the octachord, built of eight strings and encompassing the entire content of the octave. |

**Verdict.** Materies is one of four varied expressions for what these systems contain. ‘Content’ is apt here, but ‘material’ better prepares materia in X43. Both can be defended.

**Russian.** охватывающий весь материал октавы  
**English.** encompassing the entire material of the octave

Lewis and Short, s.v. materia [from memory] · Xylander X42–43 [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**A · notes · minor note-claim** · confirmed  
“the two octachords are counted separately”  
The arithmetic explains how sixteen might have arisen, but the sentence explicitly describes consecutive strings. It does not state that their common endpoint is counted twice.  
**Correction.** Present separate counting as a possible reconciliation with X15.

**C · notes · minor note-claim** · confirmed  
“the shared note is counted twice”  
This is a possible explanation for sixteen beside a fifteenth, but X42 does not explicitly count the shared note twice.  
**Correction.** Qualify the explanation and preserve the discrepancy.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · sound** · added  
**Printed.** continentib. sedecim neruis  
**Proposal.** ‘sixteen continuous strings’ / ‘sixteen successive strings’  
Read in series, the sixteen adjacent strings give a coherent sense. ‘Continuous’ should convey their arrangement, not the physical continuity of each string.  
Xylander, Basel 1556, 47.12–15, scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. continens [from memory]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Two octachords sharing their boundary yield fifteen distinct positions, yet this particular sentence says sixteen consecutive strings. The parenthetic contemporary name ‘fifteenth’ sharpens the difficulty.

#### For the editor

- Should matter/material be carried from X42 into X43, or should X42 use ‘content’ with a note?

### X43 · Xyl. 47.17–21

*Inter systemata proinde, ac in medio eorum concepta intervalla hoc interest, quod systematum omnis constitutio in materia spectatur: cogitatione autem separata materia, intervalla intelliguntur.*

**Sense.** Systems and the intervals conceived between or within them differ in this respect: the entire constitution of systems is considered in matter, while intervals are understood by mentally abstracting matter.

**Russian base · D.** Joins the physical constitution to the act of mentally abstracting from matter.

**English base · D.** Makes the contrast between the entire structure and the intervals grasped in abstraction easy to follow.

#### 1. Are intervals contained or conceived within systems?

*in medio eorum concepta intervalla*

**A, B, C** · Contained or comprised within.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Между системами, стало быть, и интервалами, заключёнными внутри них, различие то, что всякое устроение систем усматривается в материи, интервалы же мыслятся, когда материя отделена мыслью. | Between systems, accordingly, and the intervals contained in their midst there is this difference: |
| B | Итак, системы отличаются от заключённых в них интервалов тем, что всё устройство системы рассматривается в материи, а интервалы постигаются в отвлечении от материи. | Systems accordingly differ from the intervals comprised within them in that the whole constitution of a system is observed in matter, whereas intervals are understood by setting matter aside in thought. |
| C | Итак, между системами и интервалами, заключёнными внутри них, та разница, что всякое устроение систем рассматривается в материи, а интервалы постигаются, когда материя отделена мыслью. | Between systems, then, and the intervals comprised in their midst there is this difference: |

**D** · Conceived within.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Различие между системами и интервалами, которые мы мыслим внутри них, в следующем. | Systems differ from the intervals conceived within them in this respect. |

**Verdict.** Concipere allows both inclusion and mental apprehension. In medio and the enclosing language in X42 support a local sense. Cogitatione and intelliguntur favour the mental sense and let the opening phrase anticipate the conclusion. Keep the location in either case.

**Russian.** интервалы, мыслимые внутри систем  
**English.** the intervals conceived within the systems

Lewis and Short, s.v. concipio [from memory] · Xylander X42–43 [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### 2. Every instance, or the entirety of a structure?

*systematum omnis constitutio*

**A, C** · Every constitution.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Между системами, стало быть, и интервалами, заключёнными внутри них, различие то, что всякое устроение систем усматривается в материи, интервалы же мыслятся, когда материя отделена мыслью. | every constitution of systems is viewed in matter, whereas intervals are understood when matter has been set apart in thought. |
| C | Итак, между системами и интервалами, заключёнными внутри них, та разница, что всякое устроение систем рассматривается в материи, а интервалы постигаются, когда материя отделена мыслью. | every constitution of systems is viewed in matter, while intervals are understood when matter has been separated off in thought. |

**B, D** · The whole constitution.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Итак, системы отличаются от заключённых в них интервалов тем, что всё устройство системы рассматривается в материи, а интервалы постигаются в отвлечении от материи. | Systems accordingly differ from the intervals comprised within them in that the whole constitution of a system is observed in matter, whereas intervals are understood by setting matter aside in thought. |
| D | Строение системы во всей его полноте рассматривают в материи, а интервалы постигают, мысленно отвлекаясь от материи. | The whole structure of a system is considered in matter, whereas intervals are grasped by abstracting from matter in thought. |

**Verdict.** Omnis with an abstract singular can emphasize entirety or extend a statement over all cases. Singular constitutio opposite the abstract consideration of intervals and the repeated omnem in X42 favour the collective sense.

**Proposal.** …между системами и интервалами, мыслимыми внутри них… / …between systems and the intervals conceived within them…  
The mental sense is well motivated in a clause contrasting systems in matter with intervals known in thought.

- **D** · The preceding strings illustrate the material support from which an interval may be mentally abstracted.

### X44 · Xyl. 47.21–48.1

*Harmoniae proprium est, ut ea systemata construat atque concinnet ad horum melodiae (eam cantus seu cantilenae modulationem fere dixeris) generum aliquod, quae sunt diatonon, chromaticum, et harmonicum, cui ab ipsa Harmonia nomen obtigit.*

**Sense.** Harmony characteristically constructs these systems and fits them together according to one of the genera of melody. Melody might approximately be called the modulation of song or tune. The genera are diatonic, chromatic and harmonic. The last takes its name from harmony itself.

**Russian base · B.** Separates the three genera from the parenthetic gloss and identifies the final antecedent.

**English base · D.** ‘Melodic shaping’ distinguishes the explanatory term from melody while avoiding the modern sense of a key change.

#### 1. What defines melody in the parenthesis?

*cantus seu cantilenae modulationem*

**A** · A measured shaping of song or tune.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Гармонии свойственно строить и слаживать эти системы сообразно какому-либо из таких родов мелодии (её можно бы приблизительно назвать мерным ведением пения, или напева): | It is proper to harmony to construct and frame those systems according to some one of these genera of melody (you might roughly call it the measured shaping of song, or of a tune), which are the diatonic, the chromatic, and the harmonic, to which the name has fallen from Harmony itself. |

**B** · The melodic movement of singing or a tune.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Гармонии свойственно строить и стройно сочетать эти системы сообразно одному из родов мелодии (её можно приблизительно определить как мелодическое движение пения или напева). | It belongs to harmony to construct and arrange these systems according to one of the genera of melody (which one might broadly call the melodic movement of singing or of a tune). |

**C** · Russian has melodic movement, English ‘modulation’, in a historical sense explained in a note.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Свойство гармонии — строить и слаживать эти системы применительно к какому-либо из этих родов мелодии (её, пожалуй, можно было бы назвать мелодическим движением пения или напева), каковы диатонический, хроматический и гармонический, которому имя досталось от самой Гармонии. | It is proper to harmony to construct and attune those systems for some one of these genera of melody (you might almost call it the modulation of song or tune), which are the diatonic, the chromatic and the harmonic, to which [last] the name fell from Harmony itself. |

**D** · The melodic shaping or structure of singing or a tune.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Гармонии свойственно строить эти системы и согласовывать их с одним из следующих родов мелодии (мелодией, пожалуй, можно назвать мелодический строй пения или напева). | It belongs to harmony to construct these systems and fit them to one of the following genera of melody (one might perhaps describe melody as the melodic shaping of singing or of a tune). |

**Verdict.** All take cantus and cantilenae with modulationem. The pertinent sense is measured or melodic shaping of sound. Unqualified modern ‘modulation’ can evoke change of key, but C explains the older meaning. Preserve the two genitives and the provisional fere.

**Russian.** мелодический строй пения или напева  
**English.** the melodic shaping of singing or of a tune

Lewis and Short, s.v. modulatio [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.3, ed. Friedlein, p. 189 [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**A · ru · minor language** · added  
“мерным ведением пения, или напева”  
Ведение пения can suggest directing the act of singing. The gloss is about the measured musical shaping of the sound.  
**Correction.** «мерным движением пения или напева»

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** eam cantus seu cantilenae modulationem fere dixeris  
**Proposal.** ‘modulation’ or ‘melodic ordering’  
Melodic ordering and measured shaping are both defensible. Modulation is historically possible if its modern tonal meaning is excluded.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 47.21–48.1 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. modulatio [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Xylander uses diatonon in the list but diatonicum later. The genus named harmonicum corresponds to Boethius’ enharmonic, and the derivation from harmonia needs to remain audible.

#### For the editor

- Agree an English rendering for modulatio that will also work elsewhere in Xylander.

### X45 · Xyl. 48.1–7

*Nam siquidem harmonia ipsa seu consonantiarum coaptatio potissimum per tonos progrediatur, eo modo diatonicum conficitur genus melodiae: sin per semitonia, chromaticum, quasi coloratum dicas: sin ipsa harmonia diesibus maiore sui parte absolvatur, harmonicus eo modo cantus conflatur.*

**Sense.** If harmony, the fitting together of consonances, moves chiefly by tones, the diatonic genus of melody results. If by semitones, the chromatic, as if one said coloured. If harmony is for the most part realized by dieses, a harmonic song is formed.

**Russian base · B.** Makes the fitting of consonances intelligible, then distinguishes the three ways in which harmony proceeds.

**English base · D.** The three parallel conditionals and the coloured gloss are clear.

#### Corrections

**A · ru · minor language** · confirmed  
“гармония большей своей частью выполняется диесами”  
Выполняться диесами is an opaque calque for the composition of harmony from dieses.  
**Correction.** гармония по большей части складывается из диес

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · In the fourths that follow, two of the three steps are of the characteristic size. This explains ‘chiefly’ by number of steps rather than by the span they occupy.
- **C** · For the colour explanation of chromaticum compare Boethius, De musica I.21, TML, Migne text [checked].

### X46 · Xyl. 48.7–10

*Neque tamen aut solis ex tonis universa harmonia componitur, aut ex solis semitoniis, solisve diesibus: id enim fieri nequit.*

**Sense.** Harmony in its entirety cannot be composed exclusively of tones, of semitones, or of dieses.

**Russian base · D.** The three matched negatives make the exclusions clear.

**English base · D.** Accurately distributes ‘alone’ over each of the three interval classes.

### X47 · Xyl. 48.10–14

*Sed quoniam quidem tribus ex intervallis constat diatessaron, comprehensum videlicet duobus tonis et semitonio: in harmoniam coaptatis duobus tonis, opus est reliquum semitonio perfici.*

**Sense.** The fourth consists of three intervals, namely two tones and a semitone. Once two tones have been fitted into a harmony, the remainder must be completed by a semitone.

**Russian base · B.** Gives the three intervals and the act of completing the fourth clearly.

**English base · B.** The English reads continuously from the three constituent intervals to the remaining semitone.

#### 1. Does the phrase describe the fourth as comprised by or as encompassing the steps?

*comprehensum videlicet duobus tonis et semitonio*

**A, C** · The fourth is comprised or bounded by the steps.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Но поскольку кварта состоит из трёх интервалов, а именно охватывается двумя тонами и полутоном, то, когда в гармонию прилажены два тона, остальное необходимо довершить полутоном; | But since the fourth consists of three intervals, being comprised, namely, by two tones and a semitone: |
| C | Но так как кварта состоит из трёх интервалов, а именно заключена в двух тонах и полутоне, то, когда в гармонию прилажены два тона, остаток должен быть восполнен полутоном. | But since the fourth consists of three intervals, being comprised, namely, by two tones and a semitone, when two tones have been fitted into the harmony, the remainder must be completed by a semitone. |

**B** · Restates the constituents in apposition.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Но поскольку кварта состоит из трёх интервалов, а именно из двух тонов и полутона, после соединения двух тонов в гармонию остающееся [место] необходимо заполнить полутоном. | But since the fourth consists of three intervals, namely two tones and a semitone, the remainder must be filled with a semitone once two tones have been fitted into the harmony. |

**D** · The fourth encompasses or spans them.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Кварта ведь состоит из трёх интервалов и охватывает два тона и полутон. | Since the fourth consists of three intervals, spanning two tones and a semitone, once the two tones have been fitted together in harmony the remainder must be made up with a semitone. |

**Verdict.** The Latin participle is passive, agreeing with diatessaron. The two tones and semitone delimit the span of the fourth. B gives its musical content naturally, although an explicit ‘spanned by’ preserves the direction of the participle.

**Russian.** кварта охватывается двумя тонами и полутоном  
**English.** the fourth is spanned by two tones and a semitone

Lewis and Short, s.v. comprehendo [from memory] · Vitruvius, De architectura V.4.3, LacusCurtius [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 48.10–14 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · At X34 a further quantity was added. Here the two tones leave simply a semitone.

### X48 · Xyl. 48.14–16

*hocque genus diatessaron, diatonicum vocatur: quanquam ad eius constitutionem, praeter duos tonos, semitonium quoque accesserit.*

**Sense.** This genus of the fourth is called diatonic, even though a semitone as well as two tones has entered its construction.

**Russian base · D.** «В его состав … входит» expresses constitution naturally.

**English base · B.** The concessive clause identifies the semitone as a constituent despite the genus name.

#### Corrections

**D · notes · minor note-claim** · added  
“The semitone is the third.”  
This is the third item by the order of exposition. The text does not specify its position in a performance running from low to high. Vitruvius V.4.3, 6, distinguishes the steps by their bounding notes [checked].  
**Correction.** Say that one of the three steps is a semitone, or specify the order being counted.

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Boethius explains diatonic by progression through tones. The concession here acknowledges the additional semitone. Boethius, De musica I.21, TML, Migne text [checked].

### X49 · Xyl. 48.16–18

*Iam si duo sint semitonia, quod superest, ex coalitu utique conflatur intervallum, trisemitonium incompositum:*

**Sense.** With two semitones, the interval remaining is formed by coalescence: an incomposite trihemitone.

**Russian base · D.** Marks the assurance of utique and makes the interval name explicit.

**English base · D.** Keeps both fusion and the incomposite character of the step.

*utique*

**A, C, D** · Translate the assurance.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |

| D | Если же даны два полутона, то в остатке, несомненно, образуется путём слияния интервал — несоставной трёхполутон, | Now if there are two semitones, what remains is indeed an interval formed by fusion, an incomposite trihemitone, |

**B** · omits

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | — | — |

**Verdict.** Utique explicitly confirms the formation of the remaining interval; a brief ‘indeed’ is sufficient.

The assertion omits utique.

- **D** · The trihemitone equals three semitones in span but forms one performed step. Compare the chromatic tetrachord in Vitruvius V.4.3 [checked].

### X50 · Xyl. 48.19–22

*atque ita ex duobus semitoniis et trisemitonio confundetur genus diatessaron, quod Chromaticum vocatur, ad movendos affectus et luctum ciendum efficacius.*

**Sense.** The two semitones and the trihemitone thus blend to form the chromatic genus of the fourth, more effective in stirring emotions and arousing grief.

**Russian base · D.** The emotional effect and comparative degree are explicit.

**English base · D.** The blending of the intervals and the power to stir feelings and grief are clear.

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Xylander stresses efficacy in stirring emotions and grief. Vinet compares the chromatic genus to the preceding genus by its more mournful character.

### X51 · Xyl. 48.22–49.1

*Sin melodia per duas dieses procedat, alterum intervallorum erit ditonus ex coalitu: idque genus diatessaron, Harmonicum dicetur, quod eo quia sit optimum, a communi harmoniae vocabulo nomen invenit.*

**Sense.** If the melody proceeds by two dieses, the other interval is a ditone formed by coalescence. This genus of the fourth is called harmonic and, being the best, takes its name from the general word for harmony.

**Russian base · B.** Keeps the unusual «другой из интервалов» and the generic name гармония.

**English base · B.** Preserves the partitive expression and the reason given for the name.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**B · text issue · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** alterum interuallorum  
**Proposal.** Retain alterum and understand the remaining interval in context.  
The scan has alterum. After two dieses at a quarter-tone each, the 2½-tone fourth has one two-tone step left. The two distinct types of step offer a possible binary contrast, but the text does not supply the word ‘types’.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 48.22–49.1 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. alter [checked] · Vitruvius, De architectura V.4.3, LacusCurtius [checked]

**B · doubt · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** alterum intervallorum  
**Proposal.** Если же мелодия движется по двум диесам, то оставшийся интервал будет дитоном, образованным слиянием. / If melody proceeds by two dieses, the remaining interval will be a ditone formed by coalescence.  
The remaining interval is the ditone. This paraphrase captures the numerical sense while smoothing the binary form alterum.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 48.22–49.1 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. alter [checked] · Vitruvius, De architectura V.4.3, LacusCurtius [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · The referent follows from the three-step division even though the binary wording is unusual.

### X52 · Xyl. 49.1–3

*Neque alia ratione etiam ab harmonia reliqua systematum genera conficiuntur.*

**Sense.** Harmony constitutes the remaining genera of systems in just the same way.

**Russian base · B = D.** Identical wording retains harmony as agent and the same manner of composition.

**English base · B = D.** Both give an idiomatic positive equivalent of neque alia ratione and correctly retain the agent.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** ab harmonia  
**Proposal.** out of harmony  
Passive conficiuntur and the active constructing role of harmony at X44 support an agent. A material reading ‘out of harmony’ confuses who constructs the system with what it contains.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 49.1–3 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked]

### X53 · Xyl. 49.3–6

*Verum quia exemplo tetrachordi ostendimus, quomodo intervalla ipsa inter se contexerentur, facile studiosus lector etiam de reliquis systematibus coniecturam faciet.*

**Sense.** The example of the tetrachord has shown how the intervals themselves are woven together, so the diligent reader will readily infer what happens in other systems.

**Russian base · D.** Keeps the inference from a single example and the weaving image.

**English base · B.** The English retains the studious reader's inference by analogy to the other systems.

### X54 · Xyl. 49.6–10

*Sane ad cantum difficilime omnium genus melodiae harmonicum applicari potest, multumque ea res usum ad consuetudinem requirit: quare neque in usum fere venit.*

**Sense.** Of all the genera of melody the harmonic is the most difficult to apply to singing. It takes much practice to become accustomed to it, and so it hardly comes into use.

**Russian base · B.** Preserves both the difficulty in singing and the practice necessary for familiarity.

**English base · B = D.** Both distinguish the performer's practice from the general rarity of use.

#### 1. Present or perfect venit?

*neque in usum fere venit*

**A, B, D** · Present, generally hardly used.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | потому он почти и не входит в употребление. | wherefore it scarcely comes into use either. |
| B | Потому он почти не входит в употребление. | Hence it hardly comes into use. |
| D | Чтобы к нему привыкнуть, нужно долго упражняться, и потому он почти не входит в употребление. | It takes much practice to become accustomed to it, so it hardly ever comes into use. |

**C** · Perfect, has hardly come into use.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | поэтому он почти и не вошёл в употребление. | therefore it has hardly come into use. |

**Verdict.** The print does not mark the quantity of the first vowel. The parallel potest and requirit favour a general present. A resultative perfect is also grammatical and makes a historical assertion.

**Russian.** поэтому он редко употребляется  
**English.** hence it is seldom used
