### X55 · Xyl. 49.10–12

*Contra diatonicum simplex est, egregium, naturaeque aptum: quo factum est, ut Plato quoque id receperit.*

**Sense.** Conversely, the diatonic is simple, excellent and suited to nature. This is why Plato too accepted it.

**Russian base · D.** Supplies an idiomatic transition from the previous genus and retains the inference about Plato.

**English base · D.** Explicitly restores the genus as subject and gives the causal inference.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The Timaeus provides a diatonic construction. The Republic passage concerns Dorian and Phrygian harmonies. Xylander leaves Plato’s work unspecified. Plato, Timaeus 35b–36b, Jowett, MIT Classics Archive [checked]; Republic III.398e–399c [from memory].

#### For the editor

- How firmly should the commentary identify Plato’s unnamed work? Theon’s characterization and account of the world-soul support A’s identification with the Timaeus (Hiller, pp. 56.3–5, 63.25–64 and 65.1–6 [checked]). The Latin leaves the dialogue unnamed, as D observes.

### X56 · Xyl. 49.12–18

*Verum horum praeterea unumquodque variatur, inque haud paucas cantuum divisiones, differentias atque species diducitur. Modo enim Lydia, modo Phrygia, mox etiam Dorica cantilena concinnatur: ad quarum unamquamque Paean, Hymenaeus, Exodius, et alia quaecunque melodiarum genera aptantur.*

**Sense.** Each genus is further varied and divided into numerous divisions, distinctions and species of song. A Lydian, Phrygian or Dorian tune is fashioned in turn, and a paean, wedding song, exodius and other kinds of melody can be fitted to each.

**Russian base · B.** The three forms of singing are intelligible, with the last left broad.

**English base · B.** Exit song avoids restricting exodius to a specific dramatic or funerary occasion.

#### 1. Named genre, exit song, or song of farewell?

*Exodius*

**A, C** · Retain the historical name.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | и к каждому из них приспосабливаются пеан, гименей, эксодий и какие ни есть другие роды мелодий. | and to each of these the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius and whatever other kinds of melodies there are, are adapted. |
| C | и к каждому из них приспосабливаются пеан, гименей, эксодий и любые другие роды мелодий. | and to each of them the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius and whatever other genera of melodies are adapted. |

**B** · Describes the closing or exit song.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | К каждому из них прилаживают пеан, гименей, заключительную песнь и иные роды мелодии. | To each of these are fitted the paean, the hymenaeus, the exit song, and any other genera of melody. |

**D** · Russian calls it a farewell, English a song of departure.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | К каждому из них приспосабливают пеан, свадебную песнь, прощальную песнь и любые другие роды мелодий. | To each of these the paean, the wedding song, the song of departure and any other genera of melodies are fitted. |

**Verdict.** The transmitted masculine functions as a genre name in a list with Paean and Hymenaeus. An exit or closing song is a useful gloss. Farewell or burial would add an occasion the Latin does not give. Pollux records a choral song sung on departure, a specific parallel for the broad exit gloss.

**Russian.** эксодий [заключительная песнь]  
**English.** an exodius [exit song]

LSJ, s.v. ἐξόδιος [from memory] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 49.12–18 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Pollux, Onomasticon IV.108, ed. I. Bekker (1846), p. 168 [checked] · medium confidence · modified · editorial decision

#### Corrections

**D · ru · minor mistranslation** · added  
“прощальную песнь”  
The noun makes saying farewell the song’s subject or occasion. Exodius can name a song accompanying an exit or closing an action. The Latin leaves its occasion open.  
**Correction.** «эксодий» with a note ‘песнь при выходе или в завершение действия’.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**D · doubt · open** · modified · editorial decision  
**Printed.** Exodius  
**Proposal.** погребальная песнь / funeral song  
Pollux attests a song at the exit of a chorus. The Latin list gives no occasion that requires a funeral. The separate liturgical reference advanced for the funerary possibility was not verified.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 49.12–18 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · LSJ, s.v. ἐξόδιος [from memory] · Pollux, Onomasticon IV.108, ed. I. Bekker (1846), p. 168 [checked]

**A · doubt · sound** · added  
**Printed.** cantuum diuisiones, differentias atque species  
**Proposal.** divisions, differences and species of songs  
The one genitive precedes a coordinated series of three accusatives. The content of all three is supplied by the singing or songs under discussion.  
Xylander (1556), p. 49 [checked] · Allen and Greenough, Genitive with Nouns [from memory]

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · Lydian, Phrygian and Dorian qualify cantilena. The three song forms that follow can each fit any of these tunes. The print points to annotations in the margin.

#### For the editor

- Keep the masculine genre name exodius with a gloss, or use a descriptive exit/closing song?
- Does Exodius merit a historical genre label with a gloss for its possible occasions?

### X57 · Xyl. 49.18–24

*Neque enim sola intervallorum mutatione, aut eorum discrimine, sed praeterea etiam obliquioribus aut celerioribus transitibus, mansionibus, fluxibus, tum tactuum ipsorum pulsationumque variis administrationibus, innumera, atque plane varia cantilenarum genera comparantur.*

**Sense.** Innumerable and thoroughly varied kinds of tune arise from changes or differences of intervals, from more oblique or quicker transitions, stays and flows, and from the varied handling of touches and strokes themselves.

**Russian base · D.** Describes the means of performance without importing Vinet’s slower transitions.

**English base · D.** The series distinguishes a winding course, a quicker passage, resting, flowing and the performers’ touches and strokes.

#### 1. Does the opening distinguish change from difference?

*sola intervallorum mutatione, aut eorum discrimine*

**A, C, D** · Contrast change of intervals and differences between them.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Ибо не одной лишь переменой интервалов или их различием, но сверх того и более окольными или более быстрыми переходами, остановками, течениями, а также разнообразным управлением самими касаниями и ударами создаются бесчисленные и совершенно разнообразные роды напевов. | For not by the change of intervals alone, or by their distinction, but in addition also by more oblique or swifter transitions, by dwellings, by flowings, and further by the varied handling of the very touches and strokes, innumerable and altogether various kinds of tunes are brought about. |
| C | Ведь не только изменением интервалов или их различием, но, кроме того, ещё и более окольными или более быстрыми переходами, задержками, течениями, а также разнообразным обращением с самими касаниями и ударами достигаются бесчисленные и вполне разнообразные роды напевов. | For not only by the change of intervals or their distinction, but besides also by more oblique or swifter transitions, by stays, by flowings, and by various handlings of the touches themselves and of the strokes, innumerable and altogether varied genera of tunes are obtained. |
| D | Ведь бесчисленные и поистине разнообразные виды напевов возникают как от смены интервалов и различий между ними, так и от более извилистых или более быстрых переходов, остановок и непрерывного движения, а также от того, как исполняются сами прикосновения и удары. | For the countless and indeed varied kinds of tune result both from changes of interval or differences between intervals and from more winding or swifter transitions, from points of rest and passages of flowing movement, and from the various ways the touches and strokes themselves are executed. |

**B** · Russian differentiates them. English turns both into actions of varying the intervals.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Бесчисленные и весьма разнообразные роды напева получаются не только от перемены интервалов или различий между ними, но также от более непрямых или более быстрых переходов, задержек, плавных движений и от разных способов касания и удара. | Innumerable and quite varied kinds of tune are obtained by changing or varying the intervals, and also by more oblique or swifter transitions, lingering and flowing movements, and different ways of managing the touches and strokes themselves. |

**Verdict.** Mutatio is an act of changing; discrimen denotes the difference between intervals. B's English gives almost synonymous verbs and loses that distinction.

**Russian.** переменой интервалов или различиями между ними  
**English.** by changing intervals or by the differences between them

Lewis and Short, s.v. discrimen [from memory] · Xylander, Basel (1556), 49.18–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### Corrections

**B · en · minor mistranslation** · confirmed  
“by changing or varying the intervals”  
Two verbs for varying obscure the second noun's meaning of distinction or difference.  
**Correction.** by changes of interval or differences between intervals

**A · en · minor language** · confirmed  
“by dwellings, by flowings”  
These nominal plurals are opaque as descriptions of melodic performance. Mansiones denotes lingering or resting and fluxus flowing movement.  
**Correction.** by points of rest and passages of flowing movement

**C · en · minor language** · confirmed  
“by stays, by flowings, and by various handlings”  
These nominal plurals are opaque as descriptions of melodic performance. Mansiones denotes lingering or resting and fluxus flowing movement.  
**Correction.** by points of rest and passages of flowing movement

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** obliquioribus aut celerioribus transitibus  
**Proposal.** slower transitions  
Obliquioribus concerns direction or indirectness, not speed. Celerioribus introduces a second parameter. Vinet uses a different adjective.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 49.18–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. obliquus [checked]

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** tactuum ipsorum pulsationumque variis administrationibus  
**Proposal.** разнообразным управлением самими тактами и ударами / by the varied handling of the very beats and strokes  
Tactus can concern touch or the time-beat in early modern performance. Pulsationes makes instrumental contacts plausible. There is no explicit metrical unit here.  
Xylander, Basel (1556), 49.18–24 (page image and diplomatic lines) [checked] · Lewis and Short, s.v. tactus [checked]

**B · doubt · open** · added · editorial decision  
**Printed.** obliquioribus aut celeriorib. transitibus  
**Proposal.** от более обходных переходов / by more roundabout transitions  
More roundabout preserves the departure from a direct course. The adjective alone cannot determine whether this describes the melodic path or a particular performance technique. Vinet’s lentioribus supplies no textual warrant for changing the Basel word.  
Xylander (1556), p. 49 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. obliquus [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · In Xylander indirect course and greater speed are distinct. Vinet describes both alternatives in terms of speed.

#### For the editor

- Does tactus merit a note mentioning the musical time-beat alongside touch or attack?

## Vinet

### V01 · Vinet 41.1–2

*Michaelis Pselli Musica.*

**Sense.** Music of Michael Psellus, as attributed in the title.

### V02 · Vinet 41.3–4

*Caput I. De Musica in commune.*

**Sense.** Chapter I, on music in general.

**English base · A = D.** Both preserve the chapter number and the general scope of in commune.

#### Commentary to retain

- **A** · The chapter headings and numbered paragraphs belong to Vinet’s printing (project introduction §2 [checked]).

### V03 · Vinet 41.5–8

*Musica Universitatem contineri veteres dixerunt. Rerum enim naturalium nulla est, quae vacet symmetria, et proportione:*

**Sense.** The ancients said that music holds the universe together. Nothing in nature lacks symmetry and proportion.

**Russian base · D.** The active instrumental construction and the negative generalization make clear what music does for the universe.

**English base · C.** Commensurability conveys a shared measure, distinct from the following proportion.

#### 1. Which English term distinguishes the correspondence of measures from proportion?

*symmetria, et proportione*

**A** · Due measure.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | соразмерности и пропорции | due measure and proportion |

**B, D** · Symmetry in its older, proportional sense.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | соразмерности и пропорции | symmetry and proportion |
| D | соразмерности и пропорции | symmetry and proportion |

**C** · Commensurability.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | соразмерности и пропорции | commensurability and proportion |

**Verdict.** The four Russian versions agree. Vitruvius describes correspondence between parts and the whole. C foregrounds the shared measure. Historical English symmetry is defensible with a gloss. A leaves some of the relational force implicit.

**Russian.** соразмерность и пропорция  
**English.** commensurability and proportion

Vitruvius, De architectura I.2.4 (LacusCurtius Latin text), on the relation of parts to the whole [checked] · Vinet 41.5–8, supplied printing [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Explain the ancient sense of symmetria by the correspondence of the measures of parts within a whole. Vitruvius I.2.4 [checked].

#### For the editor

- Choose a single English equivalent for symmetria across V03–V05. If retaining symmetry, explain its historical sense at V03.

### V04 · Vinet 41.8–13

*quinimo eorum quae fiunt, si quid non cum apta symmetria et proportione efficitur, nunquam pulchre factum fuerit, sive artis id opus sit, sive naturae: sive sensu id percipiatur aliquo, sive sola mente comprehendi possit.*

**Sense.** Indeed, whatever comes into being without appropriate symmetry and proportion can never have been beautifully made, whether it is a work of art or of nature, perceptible to some sense or comprehensible to the mind alone.

**Russian base · B.** The two alternative pairs remain independent, both within the force of the condition.

**English base · C.** The conditional sentence preserves the two pairs of alternatives and the retrospective force of factum fuerit.

#### 1. How is Vinet’s repeated pair of technical nouns maintained in English?

*apta symmetria et proportione*

**A** · Measure and proportion, with the modifier adjusted to the clause.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | более того, если что-либо из возникающего создаётся не с надлежащей соразмерностью и пропорцией, оно никогда не окажется созданным прекрасно — будь то произведение искусства или природы, воспринимается ли оно каким-либо чувством или может быть постигнуто одним лишь умом. | indeed, if any of the things that come to be is not produced with fitting measure and proportion, it will never have been beautifully made, whether it be a work of art or of nature, and whether it be perceived by some sense or can be grasped by the mind alone. |

**B, D** · Symmetry and proportion.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | и более того, из всего, что возникает, ничто не может быть прекрасно сделано, если оно создано без должной соразмерности и пропорции, будь то дело искусства или природы, воспринимается ли оно каким-либо чувством или может быть постигнуто одним лишь умом. | and indeed, among the things that come into being, anything produced without fitting symmetry and proportion will never have been beautifully made, whether it is the work of art or of nature and whether it is perceived by any sense or can be grasped by the mind alone. |
| D | Более того, если что-либо из возникающего создаётся без надлежащей соразмерности и пропорции, оно никак не может быть создано прекрасно, будь то произведение искусства или природы, воспринимаемое каким-либо чувством или постижимое одним лишь умом. | Indeed, if anything that comes into being is produced without due symmetry and proportion, it can never have been beautifully made, whether it is the work of art or of nature, whether it is perceived by some sense or can be grasped by the mind alone. |

**C** · Commensurability and proportion.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | более того, если из того, что возникает, что-либо производится не с надлежащей соразмерностью и пропорцией, оно никогда не окажется прекрасно сделанным, будь то произведение искусства или природы, воспринимается ли оно каким-либо чувством или может быть постигнуто одним лишь умом. | indeed, if any of the things that come to be is produced without fitting commensurability and proportion, it will never prove to have been beautifully made, whether it be a work of art or of nature, whether it be perceived by some sense or can be grasped by the mind alone. |

**Verdict.** The relational sense persists from V03. C makes the common measure explicit. The historical sense of symmetry in B and D is defensible with their explanation. The Russian versions all keep соразмерность.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** ue sola mente comprehendi possit. Musi- ca autem est ipsa per se symmetria et pro- portio, ipsaq́ue adeò Vniuersitas, vtpote quæ vniuersitatis harmonia est ac conue- nientia. Quin et Deum ipsum quoque non  
**Proposal.** …и такова же сама Вселенная, поскольку музыка является гармонией и согласием Вселенной. / …and so indeed is the Universe itself, since music is the harmony and accord of the universe.  
Reading Universitas as a new subject requires two tacit changes of subject and leaves the explanation by the universe’s harmony awkward. The one copula naturally governs three predicates.  
Vinet 41.13–17, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. adeo, intensive use [checked]

**B · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** ue sola mente comprehendi possit. Musi- ca autem est ipsa per se symmetria et pro- portio, ipsaq́ue adeò Vniuersitas, vtpote quæ vniuersitatis harmonia est ac conue- nientia. Quin et Deum ipsum quoque non  
**Proposal.** и тем самым сама вселенная / and thus the universe itself  
A consequential force for adeo is possible. Following the repeated ipsa and attached -que, the emphatic addition is more idiomatic.  
Vinet 41.13–17, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. adeo [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Music is identified with the universe itself. Convenientia also starts a chain of words for accord that runs through the definition of a note.

### V06 · Vinet 41.17–42.1

*Quin et Deum ipsum quoque non inepte fortasse tum sui ipsius, tum universitatis convenientiam dixeris: in quo ita apte consistit universitas, ut perpulchre optimeque habere conspiciatur:*

### V08 · Vinet 42.2–7

**English base · D.** The three recipients and the three benefits remain distinct, and both initial connective expressions are represented.

*Nos autem hic de Musica illa, quam sensus aut potius sensuum unus auditus, percipit contemplaturque, breviter disceptabimus.*

*Sonus est vocis intervallo carentis conveniens intensio.*

**Sense.** A note is a fitting setting of the pitch of a voice which lacks interval.

**Russian base · D.** Высота expresses pitch and the bracketed clarification of agreement anticipates V15.

**English base · D.** Pitch names a musical degree accurately; capable of accord anticipates the definition’s own explanation.

#### 1. Does intensio designate tension or pitch, and what makes it conveniens?

*conveniens intensio*

**A, C** · Accordant tension, understood as the tension that determines pitch.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |

**D** · Pitch capable of according with another note.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |

**Verdict.** Both lexical paths are possible. V15 explains conveniens through possible accord with another note. A single pitch is the result in the parallel Boethian definition, making D clearest about the musical referent.

**English base · A = C.** The two translations coincide, keeping both the indefinite quaedam and the combination of several intervals.

### V15 · Vinet 42.19–23

*Sonus itaque intervallo dimensioneque vacat, ut punctum in Geometria. Conveniens vero dicitur, quod ita efferri oportet, ut cum alio sono convenire possit,*

**Sense.** A note lacks interval and dimension, like a point in geometry. It is called fitting because it must be uttered in such a way that it can agree with another note.

**Russian base · B.** Протяжённость makes the geometrical analogy clear and the purpose clause explains fitness.

**English base · C.** The verb and the adjective preserve the explicit explanation of accordant by accord.

#### 1. Does the note lack an act of measurement or measurable extension?

*intervallo dimensioneque vacat*

**A, C** · Russian измерение, English dimension.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Итак, звук не имеет интервала и измерения, как точка в геометрии. Согласным же он называется потому, что должен издаваться так, чтобы мог согласоваться с другим звуком, | The note, then, is without interval and dimension, like the point in geometry. And it is called accordant because it must be uttered in such a way that it can accord with another note, |
| C | Итак, звук не имеет ни интервала, ни измерения, как точка в геометрии. Согласующимся же он называется потому, что его следует издавать так, чтобы он мог согласоваться с другим звуком, | So a note has neither interval nor dimension, like a point in geometry. It is called accordant because it must be produced in such a way that it can accord with another note, |

**B, D** · Russian протяжённость, English dimension.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Звук, таким образом, лишён интервала и протяжённости, как точка в геометрии. Его называют пригодным для согласования, потому что издавать его следует так, чтобы он мог согласоваться с другим звуком, | A note, then, is without interval or dimension, like a point in geometry. It is called fitting because it must be produced in such a way that it can be in accord with another note, |

It is called capable of accord because it must be sounded in such a way that it can accord with another note, |

**Verdict.** The point analogy concerns extent in pitch. Измерение can be read geometrically, but протяжённость makes the absence of a traversed distance explicit. The English versions convey the same sense.

**Russian.** не имеет ни интервала, ни протяжённости

**Sense.** Unless so produced, the note is ill-sounding and out of tune, unfitting. Indeed it should by no means be called a note, but a kind of noise emitted at random, unfit for accord and concordant sounding.

**Sense.** Notes have various names. In order from low to high Vinet gives proslambanomenos, hypate hypaton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hypate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, simply mese, paramese, trite diezeugmene, diatonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite hyperbolaion, diatonos hyperbolaion and nete hyperbolaion.

**English.** simply mese

### V18 · Vinet 43.23–26

*Ex his conficitur, quod in Pentekaedecachordo est Heckaedecachordum: quando finis Octachordi, principium deinceps continuo sequentibus constituitur.*

**Sense.** From these is constituted, or from these facts follows, the sixteen-string system found in the fifteen-string system. The end of an octachord serves as beginning for the notes that follow immediately in succession.

**Russian base · A.** The numerical contrast and the shared boundary emerge clearly, with the supplied head noun marked.

**English base · C.** The declarative construction is economical syntactically and the interpretive head nouns are marked.

#### 1. Does quod introduce a conclusion or qualify the sixteen-string system?

*Ex his conficitur, quod in Pentekaedecachordo est Heckaedecachordum*

**A, B, D** · The system is composed of these notes; quod is relative.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Из них составляется та шестнадцатиструнная [система] | From these is made up |
| B | Из них образуется шестнадцатиструнная система | They form |
| D | Из них образуется шестнадцатиструнная система | From these is formed |

**C** · A conclusion follows from the foregoing; quod introduces it.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | Отсюда следует, что | From these it follows that |

**Verdict.** C gives ex his conficitur its attested inferential force and keeps both system names in the declarative clause. A relative with delayed antecedent remains possible, especially after the list of component notes. Both construals yield sixteen positions on fifteen notes.

**Russian.** Отсюда следует, что в пятнадцатиструнной [системе] заключена шестнадцатиструнная  
**English.** It follows that the fifteen-string [system] contains a sixteen-string one

**Verdict.** Both compounds can name instruments in their own right. Reading the second as the arrangement realized on the first is plausible. Mark a noun inserted specifically to impose that distinction, or translate both compounds descriptively and explain the possibilities.

**Sense.** The diesis is the smallest interval. Next comes the semitone, twice as high as the diesis, and finally the tone, consisting of two semitones. Its ratio is sesquioctave, nine to eight. The semitone is presented as eight and a half and the diesis as eight and a quarter.

### V34 · Vinet 46.13–47.7

*sed haec non sine medio, neque non-composita sunt, quando per media omnia supradicta intervalla incedunt: quae ratione quidem vocis primi soni ad ultimum, intervalla appellantur: sed a numero tamen ordineque, qui in medio sunt, sonorum, haec nomina receperunt: Dia-tessaron, ut quod per quatuor: Dia-pente, per quinque: Dia-pason per octo sonos aut chordas ingrediatur termineturque.*

**Sense.** These intervals have intervening steps and are composite, since they traverse the preceding intervals. The movement of the voice from the first note to the last makes them intervals. Their names, however, come from the number and order of the notes along the way: the fourth, fifth and octave run through and end at four, five and eight notes or strings respectively.

**Russian base · D.** D expresses the relationship of the bounding notes without deciding in advance that the comparison must be numerical.

**English base · D.** D expresses the relationship of the bounding notes without deciding in advance that the comparison must be numerical.

#### 1. Does ratio require a numerical ratio here?

*ratione quidem vocis primi soni ad ultimum*

**A, C** · Explicit numerical ratio in English, less narrowly ‘relation’ in Russian.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему | by virtue of the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last |
| C | по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему | with respect to the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last |

**B** · A sounding extending from the first note to the last.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | по звучанию от первого звука до последнего | with reference to the sounding from the first note to the last |

**D** · A relation of the sound of the first note to the last.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | по звучанию первого звука в отношении к последнему | with reference to the sound of the first note in relation to the last |

**Verdict.** D best fits the genitive primi soni and the comparative endpoint ad ultimum. The two pitches can be compared without stating a number. B makes motion explicit where the genitive more naturally specifies the first sound. Vinet does introduce numerical ratios elsewhere, so the narrower reading remains possible.

**Russian.** по соотношению звучания первого и последнего звуков  
**English.** in virtue of the relation of the first note’s sound to the last

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · open** · confirmed · editorial decision  
**Printed.** pasôn nnncupata: sed hæc non sine me- / dio, neque non-composita sunt, quando / per media omnia supradicta interualla / incedunt: quæ ratione quidem vocis pri- / mi soni ad vltimum, interualla appellan- / tur: sed à numero tamen ordinéque, qui in / medio sunt, sonorum, hæc nomina rece- / perunt: Dià-tessáron, vt quod per quatuor: / Dià-pénte, per quinque: Dià-pasôn per / octo sonos aut chordas ingrediatur termi- / neturq́ue. Dià-pasôn autem per octo di-  
**Proposal.** …интервалами они называются в рассуждении голоса от первого звука до последнего… / …they are called intervals in respect of the voice from the first note to the last…  
The ablative ratione can introduce the respect in which the name applies. Primi soni is genitive and ad ultimum marks the further term. An actual vocal passage from the first note to the last is an interpretation, not an expressed motion verb.

The ablative ratione can introduce the respect in which the name applies. Primi soni is genitive and ad ultimum marks the further term. An actual vocal passage from the first note to the last is an interpretation, not an expressed motion verb.

### V35 · Vinet 47.7–14

*Dia-pason autem per octo dicitur, tanquam in Octachordo intervallis omnibus comprehensis completisque, et hinc rursus repetitis, quemadmodum ex ipsa liquet appellationum ratione. Nam Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason posteriora intervalla vocantur.*

**Sense.** The octave is said to be through eight, as though all the intervals were embraced and completed in the octachord and repeated from there. The names of the subsequent intervals show this: octave and a fourth, octave and a fifth, double octave.

**Russian base · C.** C preserves the force of tanquam and follows the argument from the compound names.

**English base · C.** C preserves the force of tanquam and follows the argument from the compound names.

**Verdict.** The participles depend on tanquam. C most clearly preserves the qualification, which applies to the whole account of completion and repetition.

*Consonat autem intervallum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pente secundum paraphonum: Dia-pason vero, et Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason secundum antiphonum.*

**Sense.** The fourth and fifth are consonant in the paraphonic manner. The octave, the octave with fourth or fifth, and the double octave are consonant in the antiphonic manner.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · Vinet introduces two classes without the etymological glosses found in Xylander. His next paragraph illustrates antiphony with octaves.
- **A** · Boethius reports the Pythagorean objection to the octave plus fourth (8:3) and Ptolemy’s defense of its consonance (De institutione musica II.27 and V.9, ed. Friedlein [checked]).
- **B** · Pseudo-Aristotle, Problemata XIX.39, 921a [checked], explicitly calls the octave antiphonic and illustrates it with different voices singing together.
- **C** · Theon gives the same two class names and the four examples C specifies (ed. Hiller, p. 48.16–20 [checked]). Vinet also places the octave plus fourth and octave plus fifth in the antiphonic class. Gaudentius uses paraphonic for intervals between consonance and dissonance and gives the tritone and ditone as examples (Harmonic Introduction 8, ed. Jan, p. 338.3–7 [checked]).

### V37 · Vinet 47.21–48.7

*Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum et antiphonum, quod Paraphonum impari temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodammodo et apposite excipientibus inter se sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes aequabiliter: antiphonum vero aequo convenit temporis spacio, acuto cum gravi pariter consonante, ut octavo cum primo, undecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum quinto, quintodecimo cum octavo, gravibus nimirum una cum acutis, aut acutis cum gravibus peraequa proportione simul ascendentibus vel descendentibus, in intensionibus remissionibusve.*

**Sense.** In paraphony the period is unequal, the notes taking one another up smoothly and fittingly in accordance with their proportions and ratios. In antiphony the period is equal: high sounds with low, as eight with one, eleven with four, twelve with five, fifteen with eight. High and low ascend or descend together in equal proportion as pitch is raised or lowered.

**Russian base · B.** The syntax makes the successive and simultaneous relations intelligible and all four ordinal pairs survive. Retain the disjunction of raising or lowering in Russian.

**English base · B.** B preserves span, succession and simultaneity, and makes the pitch movement intelligible.

#### 1. Do the phrases specify duration or coincidence?

*impari temporis spatio … aequo … temporis spacio*

**A, C** · Unequal and equal spans in the parallel clauses.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | антифония же согласуется в равный промежуток времени | antiphony accords in an equal span of time |
| C | антифонное же согласуется в равный промежуток времени | the antiphonic accords in an equal space of time |

**B** · Unequal spans contrasted with an equal span, with simultaneous sounding specified next.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Антифония же даёт согласованность в равный промежуток времени | Antiphony accords over an equal span of time |

**D** · Different times or intervals contrasted with the same interval.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | При антифонии же звуки согласуются в один и тот же промежуток времени | they agree in the same interval of time |

**Verdict.** Preserve the grammatical comparison of spans. Excipere can indicate succession, while pariter and simul make coincidence explicit in the second half. One need not turn this into a theory of vibration periods.

**Russian.** за неодинаковый промежуток времени … за равный промежуток времени  
**English.** over an unequal span of time … over an equal span of time

### V38 · Vinet 48.8–11

*Porro tonus et hemitonium et diesis per se ipsa considerata, principium quidem consonantiae sunt, sed nondum tamen consonantia.*

**Sense.** Tone, semitone and diesis, considered in themselves, form a beginning of consonance but do not yet constitute consonance.

**Russian base · A = C.** Both express the predicative contrast between a beginning and achieved consonance, and retain the transition.

**English base · A = C.** Both express the predicative contrast between a beginning and achieved consonance, and retain the transition.

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · These smaller intervals are the starting magnitudes from which consonances are constituted.
- **C** · Theon of Smyrna (ed. Hiller, p. 49.4–5 [checked]) uses the same distinction between the beginning of consonance and consonance itself for the tone and diesis. Vinet also names the semitone.

### V39 · Vinet 48.12–20

*Conficitur autem Dia-tessaron duobus tonis et hemitonio et modica praeterea toni parte, quae diesi minor, nomine vacat: Dia-pente vero tribus tonis et hemitonio: et Dia-pason sex tonis: ac Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, tonis octo et hemitonio: Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, novem tonis et hemitonio: bis Dia-pason tonis duodecim.*

**Sense.** A fourth contains two tones, a semitone and a further unnamed part of a tone smaller than a diesis. The fifth contains three tones and a semitone, the octave six tones, the octave and fourth eight tones and a semitone, the octave and fifth nine tones and a semitone, and the double octave twelve tones.

**Russian base · B.** B keeps the extra fraction with the fourth and reproduces all six amounts without an added causal explanation.

**English base · B.** B keeps the extra fraction with the fourth and reproduces all six amounts without an added causal explanation.

**D · ru · minor addition** · confirmed  
“и потому не имеет названия”  
The relative clause states the small part’s size and lack of a name. It does not explicitly make one the cause of the other.  
**Correction.** которая меньше диесы и не имеет названия

**Sense.** The fourth has the sesquitertian ratio, the fifth the sesquialter, the octave duple, the octave and fourth duple superpartient, and the octave and fifth triple.

**English base · A = C.** The five named ratios, their brackets and the unfinished series are exact.

### V42 · Vinet 48.26–49.6

*ut exempli gratia in numeris, primus proponatur nobis terminus, numerus sex, cuius sesquitertius est plane octo: sesquialter, novem: duplex, duodecim: duplex-superpartiens, sedecim, sex bis continens, et duas eius partes: triplex, octodecim: quadruplex, viginti quatuor.*

**Sense.** Starting with six, its sesquitertian is eight, its sesquialter nine, its duple twelve, its duple superpartient sixteen (twice six and two parts of six), its triple eighteen and its quadruple twenty-four.

**Russian base · C.** C alone gives the idiomatic fractional value of duas eius partes directly. All six proportional terms and their values are correct.

**English base · C.** C alone gives the idiomatic fractional value of duas eius partes directly. All six proportional terms and their values are correct.

#### 1. Does ‘two parts’ specify a fraction?

*duas eius partes*

**A, B, D** · Literal ‘two parts’, with the fraction explained separately.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | ещё две его части | six twice and two parts of it |
| B | ещё две его доли | six twice and two of its parts besides |
| D | ещё две его части | six twice and two parts of it besides |

**C** · The idiomatic fraction two thirds.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | [ещё] две трети его | six twice and two thirds of it [besides] |

**Verdict.** The numerator-only fraction idiom supplies the denominator. The arithmetic independently fixes it: 16 − 12 = 4 = two thirds of six. C gives the full value without a gloss.

**Russian.** две трети его  
**English.** two thirds of it

### V43 · Vinet 49.7–10

*Verumtamen sciendum, rationes intervallorum, non ex sonorum, aut ex chordarum numero iudicari; sed ex intentione vocis a gravi ad acutius:*

**Sense.** Interval ratios are judged from the raising of the voice from low to higher pitch. The number of notes or strings does not supply that measure.

**Russian base · D.** D renders the comparison from low to higher as an audible rise in pitch and preserves the contrast with counting.

**English base · D.** D renders the comparison from low to higher as an audible rise in pitch and preserves the contrast with counting.

#### 1. Is intentio physical tension or rise of pitch?

*ex intentione vocis a gravi ad acutius*

**A, C** · Tension of the voice.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому | the tension of the voice from low to higher |
| C | по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому | the tension of the voice from low to higher |

**B, D** · Rise of the voice or sound in pitch.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | по повышению звучания от низкого к более высокому | the rise in pitch of the sound from low to higher |
| D | по повышению голоса от низкого звучания к более высокому | the rise of the voice from a low pitch to a higher one |

**Verdict.** The noun admits tension, but the two pitch endpoints specify what is measured. The rise in pitch also explains why the count of notes cannot determine the ratio.

**Russian.** по повышению голоса от низкого звука к более высокому  
**English.** by the rise of the voice from low to higher pitch

**C · notes · major note-claim** · added  
“intentio being the classical form and intensio the late one”  
Intensio already occurs in first-century imperial authors. Lewis & Short, s.v. intensio, cites Scribonius Largus, Compositiones 255, and Seneca, Naturales quaestiones VII.1.3 [checked]. The later musical citation does not date the noun itself.  
**Correction.** Distinguish the printed forms here and at V45 without assigning intensio as a word to late Latin.

**A · text issue · sound** · modified

**Proposal.** Keep intentione as printed; treat it as a spelling variant of intensione.  
Retain the printed intentio-/ne. Both nouns belong to intendo and overlap in vocal and pitch contexts. Latin lexica give them separate entries with distinct ranges of meaning; treating every occurrence as merely a spelling variant would go beyond the evidence here.

### V44 · Vinet 49.10–18

*quandoquidem intervallum Dia-pente, cuius est ratio sesquialtera, sesquialterum minime videtur ex ratione numeri sonorum, quo numero superat sonos, qui ipsum praecedunt. Nam primi soni quincuplex invenitur: secundi vero, duplum sesquialterum: tertii, superpartiens: quarti, sesquiquartum: nullius vero sesquialterum demonstrari potest.*

**Sense.** Although the fifth has sesquialter ratio, its position among the notes does not yield that ratio. Compare the fifth with the preceding notes: relative to the first it is quintuple, to the second what Vinet calls duplum sesquialterum, to the third superpartient, to the fourth sesquiquartan. With none is it sesquialter.

**Russian base · B.** The comparison between numbered positions is intelligible throughout, including the unusual ratio names.

**English base · D.** The bracketed fifth note makes the numerical argument transparent while preserving the unusual compound ratio name.

#### 1. Is the number of notes itself a ratio?

*ex ratione numeri sonorum*

**A, B, D** · Reckoning by numbered positions.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | если считать по числу звуков | on a reckoning by the number of the notes |
| B | по счёту звуков | by the count of notes |
| D | если исходить из числа звуков | if judged by the number of the notes |

**C** · The ratio of the number of notes.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | по отношению числа звуков | from the ratio of the number of notes |

**Verdict.** The parallel ex … numero in V43 and a … numero in V45 resolves ratio here as a reckoning. Two numbers enter a ratio only when the fifth position is actually compared with another.

**Russian.** по счёту звуков  
**English.** on a reckoning by the number of notes

**C · notes · major note-claim** · added  
“The argument is decisive only for the fifth”  
Even under C’s test another listed case works. The double octave ends at position 15, and no integral position k has 15:k = 4:1, since k would be 15/4. The eleventh likewise has no integral k with 11:k = 8:3.  
**Correction.** Say that some position ratios coincide accidentally with consonant ratios, and omit the claim of uniqueness.

### V47 · Vinet 50.3–11

*Inventae autem hae sunt intervallorum rationes et deprehensae, ex dispari chordarum vel longitudine vel crassitudine: vel ea, quae per torsionem claviculorum fit, intensione; aut, quod compertum magis, ex ponderum e nervis appensione: In iis vero quae inflata sonant, aut ex fistularum amplitudine, aut ex intensione spiritus ac remissione.*

**Sense.** The ratios were discovered from differences in string length, thickness or tension produced by turning the pegs. A more firmly established means was hanging weights from strings. With wind instruments the sources were the size of pipes or the strengthening and slackening of the breath.

**Russian base · D.** The vocabulary distinguishes the tension of a string from the force of the breath, and keeps the pipe dimension general.

**English base · D.** The vocabulary distinguishes the tension of a string from the force of the breath, and keeps the pipe dimension general.

| --- | --- | --- |
| A | по ширине трубок | the width of the pipes |
| C | по ширине трубок | the width of the pipes |

**B, D** · Size or dimensions of pipes.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | по величине трубок | the dimensions of the pipes |
| D | по величине трубок | the size of the pipes |

**Verdict.** Amplitudo admits width, but nothing in the clause fixes a particular dimension or specifies the internal bore. Size preserves the scope.

**Russian.** по величине трубок  
**English.** from the size of the pipes

### V49 · Vinet 50.14–51.3

*Systematum autem hoc quidem Tetrachordum est, quod omnes intervalli Dia-tessaron continet facultates: aliud vero Pentachordum, quod intervalli Dia-pente facultates complectitur: aliud Octachordum, quod totam intervalli Dia-pason vim continet: aliud deinceps Heckaedecachordum, bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem, duplicesque habens facultates: eas enim, quas modo diximus, atque intervallorum Dia-pason Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason facultates in se continet.*

**Sense.** The tetrachord contains all the capacities of the fourth, the pentachord those of the fifth, the octachord the whole power of the octave. Next the sixteen-string system takes in the full upward extent of the double octave and has two sets of capacities: those already named and those of the octave and fourth, octave and fifth, and double octave.

**Russian base · B.** B makes the extent of the double octave and the two sets of capacities clear.

**English base · D.** D preserves the difference between facultates and vis and the sense of the double octave as a pitch span.

#### 1. What does the system encompass?

*bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem*

**A, C** · The entire tension of the double octave.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | всё напряжение двойной октавы | the entire tension of the double octave |
| C | всё напряжение двойной октавы | the entire tension of the double octave |

**B** · The full pitch range.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | всю высотную протяжённость двойной октавы | the full pitch range of the double octave |

**D** · The entire rise in pitch.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | весь подъём на двойную октаву | the full rise of the double octave |

**Verdict.** The parallel descriptions give the compass of each system. The double octave supplies an extent of pitch, whereas Vinet explicitly says what is physically tightened in V47. B and D express the relevant measurement.

**Russian.** охватывающая весь диапазон двойной октавы  
**English.** spanning the full pitch range of the double octave

**A · both · minor language** · modified

The modern expression “tension of the double octave” obscures the compass of a system. The accompanying commentary identifies the intended musical sense, so this literal rendering need not imply a claim about mechanical string tension.

“всё напряжение двойной октавы / the entire tension of the double octave”  
The modern expression “tension of the double octave” obscures the compass of a system. The accompanying commentary identifies the intended musical sense, so this literal rendering need not imply a claim about mechanical string tension.  
**Correction.** весь диапазон двойной октавы / the full pitch span of the double octave

#### Commentary to retain

- **C** · Vinet retains the Greek instrument name and puts two interval names side by side without cum. Xylander here describes the strings.

### V50 · Vinet 51.4–7

*Differunt autem Systemata ab intervallis quae in ipsis sunt, quod illa in materia consistunt: intervalla vero a materia abstracta consideramus.*

**Sense.** Systems subsist in matter. The intervals contained in them, by contrast, we consider in abstraction from matter.

**Russian base · B = D.** Both identify the systems as the referent of illa and distinguish their mode of existence from our manner of considering intervals.

**English base · B = D.** Both identify the systems as the referent of illa and distinguish their mode of existence from our manner of considering intervals.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The strings give a concrete example of matter. Their pitch interval can be considered in abstraction.

### V51 · Vinet 51.8–9

*Caput V. De Harmonia.*

**Sense.** Chapter V, on harmony.

**Russian base · A = B = C = D.** All preserve the chapter number and the technical noun.

**English base · A = B = C = D.** All preserve the chapter number and the technical noun.

### V52 · Vinet 51.10–15

*Harmoniae porro proprium est Systemata illa componere aptareque ad aliquod genus modulationis, ut ad Diatonon, ad Chromaticon, et ad id quod ab ipsa harmonia cognominatum, Harmonicum dicimus.*

**Sense.** Harmony has the special function of assembling and fitting these systems to a genus of melodic performance, such as the diatonic, the chromatic and the one called harmonic after harmony itself.

**Russian base · D.** D preserves both actions and the explanation of the name harmonic, in a natural sentence.

**English base · D.** D preserves both actions and the explanation of the name harmonic, in a natural sentence.

#### 1. Is modulatio a melody or the activity of melodic movement?

*genus modulationis*

**A, C, D** · A genus of melody.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | к какому-либо роду мелодии | to some genus of melody |
| C | к какому-либо роду мелодии | to some genus of melody |
| D | к какому-либо роду мелодии | to some genus of melody |

**B** · A genus of melodic movement.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | к какому-либо роду мелодического движения | to some genus of melodic movement |

**Verdict.** The noun can denote either musical execution or its result. Here the genera are classes of melody. B’s process-oriented term is defensible, though heavier and less apt for the countable song types of V64.

**Russian.** род мелодии  
**English.** genus of melody

Vinet 51.10–15, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. modulatio [checked] · high confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The conventional harmonic preserves Vinet’s derivation from harmony. This genus is usually called enharmonic.

### V53 · Vinet 51.15–20

*Si igitur tonis plurimum harmonia peragitur, Diatonon sic vocatum efficitur genus: si Hemitoniis, Chromaticum: si vero diesibus fere harmonia continetur, conficitur, quam Harmonicam appellant modulationem.*

**Sense.** If harmony proceeds principally by tones the diatonic genus results. With semitones it is the chromatic. If it consists mostly of dieses the resulting melody is called harmonic.

**Russian base · D.** D preserves both qualifications of predominance and the switch from genus to a melody at the end.

**English base · D.** D preserves both qualifications of predominance and the switch from genus to a melody at the end.

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** nicum dicimus. Si igitur tonis plurimùm / harmonia peragitur, Diátonon sic voca- / tum efficitur genus: si Hemitoniis, Chro- / maticum: si verò diesibus ferè harmonia / continetur, conficitur, quam Harmonicam / appellant modulationem.  
**Proposal.** is held together almost wholly by dieses  
Fere can have an approximate sense, but ‘almost wholly’ overstates it here. In V59 the remaining ditone takes most of the span. Two of the three steps are dieses.  
Vinet 51.15–20, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. fere [checked] · Vitruvius, De architectura V.4.3 [checked]

- **C** · In each of the three divisions the characteristic interval occurs in two out of three steps.

### V54 · Vinet 51.21–52.1

*Verumenimvero neque omnino tonis, quae ad Harmoniam pertinent, efferuntur, neque hemitoniis omnino, neque omnino diesibus: hoc enim fieri non potest:*

**Sense.** What belongs to harmony cannot be expressed wholly in tones, wholly in semitones or wholly in dieses.

**Russian base · B.** The repeated restriction to a single kind of step is clear, as is the impossibility of such a performance.

**English base · B.** The repeated restriction to a single kind of step is clear, as is the impossibility of such a performance.

### V55 · Vinet 52.1–6

*quinimo, quoniam ex tribus intervallis Dia-tessaron intervallum confectum est, duobusque tonis et hemitonio comprehensum, necesse est, postquam duo prima intervalla imposueris, reliquum esse Hemitonium:*

**Sense.** The fourth is made up of three intervals and spans two tones and a semitone. Once one has laid down the first two intervals, a semitone must remain.

**Russian base · D.** D keeps the three steps distinct from the sizes that fill their span and does not insert a missing specification of the first two.

**English base · D.** D keeps the three steps distinct from the sizes that fill their span and does not insert a missing specification of the first two.

#### 1. Does the clause contradict V54 or establish its assertion?

*quinimo*

**A, C** · On the contrary.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | напротив | rather |
| C | напротив | on the contrary |

**B** · Indeed or moreover.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | Более того | Indeed |

**D** · Indeed in English, explanatory ведь in Russian.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D | Ведь | Indeed |

**Verdict.** The contrast rejects the possibility of a fourth made entirely of one step size. V55 supplies the proof for V54’s negative assertion. A positive transition makes this continuity clearer; the corrective sense of quinimo can also reach back to the rejected possibility.

**Russian.** В самом деле, поскольку…  
**English.** Indeed, since…

*atque hoc fuerit Dia-tessaron Diatonon cognominatum genus, ex Hemitonio et tonis constans duobus.*

**Sense.** This will be the genus of the fourth called diatonic, consisting of a semitone and two tones.

**Russian base · A = C.** These versions retain the order semitone, tone, tone and the name of the genus.

**English base · A = C = D.** These versions retain the order semitone, tone, tone and the name of the genus.

**Sense.** If two intervals are semitones, there will remain a single interval formed by combination, a simple trihemitone.

**Russian base · C.** C preserves the terms for fusion and simplicity and uses brackets for the supplied participle in each language.

**English base · C.** C preserves the terms for fusion and simplicity and uses brackets for the supplied participle in each language.

*Trihemitonium simplex*

### V58 · Vinet 52.11–14

*eritque id Dia-tessaron genus Chromaticum, ex Hemitoniis duobus et Trihemitonio temperatum, quod priore moestius luctuosiusque est.*

**Sense.** This will be the chromatic genus of the fourth, blended from two semitones and a trihemitone. It is sadder and more mournful than the preceding genus.

**Russian base · D.** The separate sentence makes genus the unmistakable antecedent of the comparative judgment.

**English base · D.** The separate sentence makes genus the unmistakable antecedent of the comparative judgment.

- **D** · Vinet attributes the mournful quality to the genus, whereas Xylander describes an effect on the listener.

### V59 · Vinet 52.14–20

*Si vero per duas dieses modulatio peragatur, quod reliquum est intervallum, erit unum ditonium ex confusione, ac Dia-tessaron hoc genus Harmonicum nominatur, quod sit optimum, ab ipsa communi omnium harmonia cognominatum.*

**Sense.** If the melody proceeds by two dieses, the remaining interval will be one ditone resulting from combination. This genus of the fourth is called harmonic because it is the best, taking its name from harmony common to them all.

**Russian base · D.** D marks the supplied genera, conveys the reported valuation in quod sit and follows the sequence of the argument.

**English base · D.** D marks the supplied genera, conveys the reported valuation in quod sit and follows the sequence of the argument.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |

**Verdict.** The genera are the immediate subject and V52 already makes the name refer to harmony. V05 gives a possible wider resonance. B and D aptly mark their supplement, but A preserves the openness of the expression.

**Russian.** от самой гармонии, общей для всех  
**English.** from harmony itself, common to all

Vinet 52.14–20, supplied printing [checked] · Vinet I.1 and V.1–2 [checked] · medium confidence · confirmed · editorial decision

**Verdict.** The subjunctive naturally presents the grounds on which the name is bestowed. The affirmative reason is possible in fluent translation, but C and D retain the distance conveyed by sit.

### V60 · Vinet 52.21–22

*Eodem modo et caetera quoque systemata constituit harmonia, temperatque.*

**Sense.** Harmony also constitutes and tempers the other systems in the same manner.

**Russian base · D.** The two verbs distinguish forming the systems from proportioning their constituents.

**English base · D.** The two verbs distinguish forming the systems from proportioning their constituents.

### V61 · Vinet 52.23–27

*Ex proposito enim exemplo, quo ostensum in uno tetrachordo, quomodo intervalla intervalli Dia-tessaron constituantur, facile studiosus alia systemata quomodo componantur, assequi poterit.*

**Sense.** The example of the disposition of the intervals of the fourth in a single tetrachord will enable the student to understand how the other systems are put together.

**Russian base · D.** D distinguishes the intervals composing the fourth from the tetrachord that illustrates them.

**English base · B.** B expresses the nested intervals without obscuring what the student is to infer.

### V62 · Vinet 53.1–4

*Genus autem Harmonicum est modulatu difficillimum, meditatione multa exercitationeque indigens: unde eius est usus rarior.*

**Sense.** The harmonic genus is exceedingly difficult to perform melodically and requires much study and practice. Hence its use is comparatively rare.

**Russian base · B.** B gives the respect in which the genus is difficult and separates preparation from practice.

**English base · B.** B gives the respect in which the genus is difficult and separates preparation from practice.

#### Commentary to retain

- **B** · The supine specifies the activity in which the difficulty lies. Meditatio and exercitatio denote preparation and practice.

### V63 · Vinet 53.4–6

*Diatonum vero est simplum et generosum et magis naturale: quare id et Plato admittit.*

**Sense.** The diatonic genus is simple, noble and more natural. Accordingly Plato too admits it.

**Russian base · D.** D marks the contextual noun, preserves the comparative and gives et its additive sense before Plato.

**English base · D.** D marks the contextual noun, preserves the comparative and gives et its additive sense before Plato.

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · Distinguish the division in Plato’s Timaeus from the selection of modes in Republic III. Vinet names no dialogue.
- **C** · Theon (ed. Hiller, p. 56.3–5 [checked]) gives the same characterization of the diatonic genus. His later account links Plato’s diatonic division to a compass of 27:1 and to the harmonic construction of the world-soul (pp. 63.25–64 and 65.1–6 [checked]), grounding the identification with the Timaeus.

### V64 · Vinet 53.7–14

*Horum autem unumquodque rursus variatur, et in multas divisiones et differentias et genera modulationum distribuitur: et modo Lydius, modo Phrygius, modo Dorius appellatur modulus: a quorum quolibet Paeeon, Hymenaeus, Exodius, et quotquot sunt modulationum genera, existunt.*

**Sense.** Each of these is subdivided into many divisions, distinctions and kinds of melody. The tune or mode is called Lydian, Phrygian or Dorian, and from any of these arise the paean, the wedding song, the exodius and every other kind of melody.

**Russian base · D.** D clearly distinguishes the genera, modes and song types and conveys quolibet. Change the farewell song to a song of departure.

**English base · D.** D distinguishes genera, modes and song types and preserves the indefinite force of quolibet. Keep the Latin song titles in the note.

#### 1. How much should the song titles identify their occasions?

*Paeeon, Hymenaeus, Exodius*

**A, C** · Transliterates the three titles.

| --- | --- | --- |

| --- | --- | --- |
| D | пеан, свадебная песнь, прощальная песнь | the paean, the wedding song, the song of departure |

**Verdict.** The parallel list names song types. A readable description works for hymenaeus and exodius, provided the latter is not confined without evidence to a particular rite. Retain the printed forms in commentary. Pollux records a choral song sung on departure, a specific parallel for the broad exit gloss.

#### Corrections

**C · notes · major note-claim** · added  
“masculine, in agreement with Paeeon and Hymenaeus”  
Three coordinated names need not agree in gender with one another. Any agreement explaining an adjectival ending would be with an expressed or understood substantive.  
**Correction.** Describe Exodius as a masculine song name or a substantivized adjective; leave its history open.

**D · ru · minor mistranslation** · added

Russian introduces a farewell address. Exodius names a song connected with going out or departure. Pollux IV.108, ed. Bekker, p. 168 [checked], illustrates a chorus leaving.  
**Correction.** песнь при выходе

**Printed.** Horum autem vnumquodque rursus / variatur, et in multas diuisiones et diffe- / rentias et genera modulationum distribui / tur: et modò Lydius, modò Phrygius, mo- / dò Dorius appellatur modulus: à quorum / quolibet Pæéon, Hymenæus, Exodius, et / quotquot sunt modulationum genera, exi / stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu-  
**Proposal.** погребальная песнь / funeral song  
Pollux attests a song at the exit of a chorus. The Latin list gives no occasion that requires a funeral. The separate liturgical reference advanced for the funerary possibility was not verified.  
Vinet 53.7–14, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. exodium [from memory] · Pollux, Onomasticon IV.108, ed. I. Bekker (1846), p. 168 [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · Vinet distinguishes the mode from the kinds of song that can arise from any one mode. The three names do not form matching pairs.

#### For the editor

- Should the song types be printed as technical names with glosses or translated by their occasions? Exodius leaves the occasion open.

### V65 · Vinet 53.14–20

*Non enim sola intervallorum mutatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitionibus, pausis quoque et fluxibus, atque variis tactibus pulsibusque, pro vario manus temperamento, infinitae et diversae modulationes conficiuntur.*

**Sense.** Countless different melodies are produced by changes and differences of intervals, by slower and animated transitions, pauses and flows, and by varied touches and strokes according to the differing control of the hand.

**Russian base · D.** D distinguishes pauses from flowing movement and connects the varied attacks to the player's control.

**English base · D.** D distinguishes pauses from flowing movement and connects the varied attacks to the player's control.

#### 1. How does the hand govern touches and strokes?

*pro vario manus temperamento*

**A, B, D** · The hand varies or controls its execution, with A’s Russian speaking of the measure of its movement.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | сообразно различной мере [движения] руки | according to the varying control of the hand |
| B | в зависимости от того, как действует рука | depending on how the hand is controlled |
| D | смотря по тому, как ими управляет рука | according to the varying control of the hand |

**C** · The ‘measure of the hand’ in English, explained as a measure of movement in Russian.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C | сообразно различной мере [движений] руки | according to the varied measure of the hand |

**Verdict.** Temperamentum concerns regulation and due proportion. The context makes the manner of handling the succession of touches and strokes explicit. C’s English can suggest a physical measurement of the hand.

**Russian.** смотря по тому, как рука соразмеряет движения  
**English.** according to the varying control exercised by the hand

Vinet 53.14–20, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. temperamentum [checked] · high confidence · confirmed

#### 2. Are both adjectives explicitly comparative?

*lentioribus, et concitatis transitionibus*

**A** · Slower transitions and rapid transitions in both versions.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | более медленными и стремительными переходами | slower and by rapid transitions |

**B, C, D** · Russian retains the asymmetry. English supplies a comparative for the second adjective.

| Version | Russian wording | English wording |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | более медленных и стремительных переходов | slower and more rapid transitions |
| C | более медленными и стремительными переходами | slower and quicker transitions |
| D | то более медленные, то стремительные | slower and more animated transitions |

**Verdict.** Only lentioribus is morphologically comparative. The coordinated concitatis denotes brisk or animated execution. A parallel English comparative is intelligible but less exact.

**Russian.** более медленными и стремительными переходами  
**English.** by slower and rapid transitions

Vinet 53.14–20, supplied printing [checked] · Allen and Greenough §§124–125, comparative formation [from memory] · high confidence · added

#### Corrections

**C · en · minor language** · confirmed  
“according to the varied measure of the hand”  
‘Measure of the hand’ does not convey the regulation of a player’s movement in idiomatic English.  
**Correction.** according to the varying control of the hand

**A · both · minor language** · added  
“паузами и течениями / pauses too and flowings”  
The bare plurals течения and flowings do not idiomatically denote continuous melodic movement in this contrast with pauses.  
**Correction.** паузами и непрерывным движением / pauses and passages of continuous movement

**C · both · minor language** · added  
“паузами и течениями / pauses too and flowings”  
The bare plurals are obscure in the musical context. C’s note correctly supplies the sense of continuity.  
**Correction.** паузами и непрерывным движением / pauses and passages of continuous movement

#### Textual proposals and doubts

**A · doubt · unsound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu- / tatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed / etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitio- / nibus, pausis quoqne et fluxibus, atque va- / riis tactibus pulsibúsque, pro vario manus / temperamento, infinitæ et diuersæ mo- / dulationes conficiuntur.  
**Proposal.** …сообразно различной настройке, [производимой] рукой… / …according to the varied tuning by the hand…  
The clause follows touches and strokes. A varying manner of controlling these movements is intelligible without introducing a separate act of tuning a string or instrument.  
Vinet 53.14–20, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. temperamentum [checked]

**C · doubt · sound** · confirmed  
**Printed.** stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu- / tatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed / etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitio- / nibus, pausis quoqne et fluxibus, atque va- / riis tactibus pulsibúsque, pro vario manus / temperamento, infinitæ et diuersæ mo- / dulationes conficiuntur.  
**Proposal.** смотря по различному владению рукой / according to the varied management of the hand  
‘Management of the hand’ identifies regulation in performance and avoids the physical measurement suggested by ‘measure of the hand’.  
Vinet 53.14–20, supplied printing [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. temperamentum [checked]

#### Commentary to retain

- **D** · The touches and strokes involve the performing hand. Vinet leaves the instrument unspecified.

### V66 · Vinet 53.21

*Finis Musicae.*

**Sense.** End of the music section.

**Russian base · C = D.** Both retain the short closing formula, treating Music as the title.

**English base · C = D.** Both retain the short closing formula, treating Music as the title.

### accessio ad acutum · xylander

X18

| Translator | Russian | English |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | продвижение к высокому | advance toward the high |

### genus / species (classification) · xylander

X15, X18, X26, X31, X52, X56, X57

| Translator | Russian | English |

**Recommended** · род / вид / kind / species  
In the initial classifications preserve what is being counted. At X56 species divides cantus into forms, while genera identifies song kinds. The distinct technical names of the three melodic genera are treated separately. Vinet uses different classifying nouns in his account of subdivisions.

### Inconsistencies in terminology

**B · convenientia.** The Russian cosmic passages V05, V06 and V08 use согласованность. The same abstract noun at V16 becomes согласование, suggesting the act of bringing into agreement. Retain a noun for the state there, especially as V16 deliberately recalls the opening.

**B · proportionalitas.** X04 follows B’s listed пропорциональность / proportionality, but X32 gives пропорциях / proportions for proportionalitates. The plural alone does not explain using the ordinary X02–03 equivalent of proportio here. In X32 Xylander expressly pairs proportionalitates with rationes. Compare Boethius, De institutione musica II.12 [checked].

**B · vis / facultas.** V32 renders vis by force. In V49 the English capacity serves for both vis and facultas, though the Russian retains сила for vis. Distinguish the near-synonyms at V49 as the Latin does.

**D · proportionalitas.** D distinguishes пропорция / proportion from пропорциональность / proportionality in X02–04 and in the term list. The X32 text then gives пропорциях / proportions for proportionalitates, obscuring the distinction without explaining the change. Compare Boethius, De institutione musica II.12 [checked].

## Overall assessment

### Translator A

**Strengths.** A gives the English editor a good starting point at X13. The change from what a sound might be to what it will be called survives, as does the distinction between a musical note and random noise. The Russian at X26 preserves Xylander’s explicit introduction of composite intervals. A’s commentary also clarifies difficult arithmetic. At X36 and V42 it shows why sixteen contains six twice with two thirds of six left over, and distinguishes that whole ratio from the ratio that follows from a single whole plus two thirds.

**Weaknesses.** A sometimes carries the form of the Latin into a modern language at the expense of the sense. At V27 the English says that eight cannot be squared, although the point is that eight is not itself a square number. At V45 the reader has to infer pitch from a bare adjective. The positive cent value in the V22 check belongs to the reciprocal of the indicated ratio. At X38 A’s running translation depends on a conjectural relative pronoun, and the grammatical defense confuses the possible antecedents. The X14 and V17 notes also overstate what distinguishes the name of mese in the lists.

### Translator B

**Strengths.** B is especially clear where the argument depends on the direction of a comparison. Both languages preserve the sequence of ratios at V19, the fractional scale values at V22 and the progression from six at X36. The V42 diagram note distinguishes the five printed numerals from the six lines they help explain. The note at X14 helps readers identify unfamiliar pitches while preserving differences between the two lists.

**Weaknesses.** Where the naming or the syntax is itself the subject, B can condense too far. Part of X28’s comparison between Greek names and ordinary Latin names becomes tautological in English. The Russian at V09 turns hearing into the human observer’s instrument, and at V14 the wording suggests making the systems that Vinet says are themselves combined. At V32 the language of staying within an interval’s limits obscures the point that the listener hears a single step. At X32 the plural ‘proportions’ also loses B’s earlier distinction between proportio and proportionalitas.

### Translator C

**Strengths.** C preserves distinctions on which the argument turns. The English at X02 and V03–V05 makes the common measure audible in two different Latin terms. At V42 both languages make the fractional idiom intelligible, and the Boethian references substantiate it. At V57 both the fusion of the constituents and the description of the result as simple remain in the sentence. C’s comparisons with other theorists are particularly helpful at V38 and V63, where their scope can be checked against the cited passages.

**Weaknesses.** C’s explanations sometimes assert more than their evidence supports. At V30 the note identifies a half interval’s ratio with the intermediate length, although the numerical check distinguishes the two. At V31 the note assigns incomposita to the point at which Xylander actually writes compositae. The dating of intensio at V43 is too late, and the note at V46 overlooks the double octave and the eleventh as further decisive tests. At V64 the gender of the three coordinated genre names does not justify the proposed grammatical agreement.

### Translator D

**Strengths.** D often supplies a clear running text where a close rendering could obscure the action. At X09 the second expression explains what the mutual relation of notes amounts to. At X42 the description retains sixteen consecutive strings and the name ‘fifteenth’ together. At V14 the systems are the constituents of harmony, and at V43 and V49 pitch movement and compass are intelligible. At V65 the English and Russian make the player’s changes of movement perceptible.

**Weaknesses.** Several local glosses introduce implications the editor should remove. At X30 the English ‘two eighths’ can suggest fractions or note values. At V39 the Russian makes the unnamed fraction’s smallness the cause of its having no name. At V42 D reports a printed 3 on a line whose three divisions can be counted but whose numeral is absent. At X56 and V64 the Russian specifies a farewell where the genre name establishes departure. At X32 ‘proportions’ blurs the distinction D established in the opening.

D is a frequent choice for running prose in both languages. Individual passages call for a different base, including A’s English at X13, B’s account of the fractional scale at V22, and C’s account of 8:3 at V42. Keep the two Latin arguments separately visible. Xylander supplies ordinary Latin interval names and glosses his terms for sounding alongside or opposite. Vinet introduces his two classes without those etymologies. X26 calls the interval species composite before X27 calls an individual interval incomposite, while V31 introduces the list as simple. The scale examples also require a distinction between operations. Starting from eight, adding one half or one quarter of a unit gives the ratios 17:16 and 33:32 to the initial length. Equal divisions of the musical interval 9:8 give its square root or fourth root. Finally, X34 gives an unnamed quantity smaller than a diesis, V39 explicitly calls it part of a tone, and V55 subsequently reckons the fourth without it. Preserve each statement and explain the tension in the commentary. The segment recommendations give separate choices for Russian and English wherever their merits differ.

**Contamination.** none
